Our aim is to demonstrate how the method works using simple, pedagogical examples and explore the type of regions that are generated in this expansion, noting the special nature of semi-infinite Wilson lines. To this end we will examine the regions in both parameter space and momentum space.

We begin by considering the simplest example of a one-loop computation and then proceed to our main case study, namely the computation of the soft AD
\Omega_{(IJk)}
, namely tripole corrections arising from the interaction between two timelike Wilson lines (
I
and
J
) and a single lightlike line (
k
), through two loops. The latter was first determined in ref.
Ferroglia:2009ep
by expanding the fully timelike result.

Given that two-loop webs can connect at most three Wilson lines, it will be sufficient to work with correlators of three lines at general angles (i.e. without imposing momentum conservation). We denote the lines
I
,
J
and
K
, where line
K
will be singled out as the one which approaches the lightlike limit,
\beta_{K}^{2}\to 0
. For convenience we associate the index
k
to the corresponding velocity after taking the limit, i.e.
\beta_{k}^{2}=0
.

As explained above, we will initially set all three lines,
I
,
J
and
K
, timelike, and then perform the asymptotic expansion in the lightlike limit
\beta_{K}^{2}\rightarrow\beta_{k}^{2}=0
. Importantly, we will perform the expansion in Euclidean regime
\{\alpha_{IJ}>0,\alpha_{IK}>0,\alpha_{JK}>0\}
(recall the definition in eq. (
14
)) where the second Symanzik polynomials of all the integrals appearing in this section are positive definite. Besides the convenience of computing integrals, the key advantage is that the geometric method of determining the regions is then guaranteed to give the complete set, i.e. no hidden regions exist. In the
\beta_{K}^{2}\to 0
limit, the Euclidean regime becomes
\{y_{IJk}>0,\alpha_{IJ}>0\}
. In practice, we will use the following parametrization and approach the limit by sending
\lambda
to be small,

\alpha_{IK}=\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}},\qquad\quad\alpha_{JK}=\lambda\sqrt{y_{IJk}},

(60)

while
\alpha_{IJ}
remains
{\cal O}(\lambda^{0})
, where we recall that the variables are defined in eq. (
14
) and eq. (
11
). In terms of
\lambda^{2}
, the nearly lightlike

\beta_{K}^{2}
becomes

\beta_{K}^{2}=\frac{(2\beta_{I}\cdot\beta_{k})(2\beta_{J}\cdot\beta_{k})}{\sqrt{\beta_{I}^{2}\beta_{J}^{2}}}\lambda^{2}+{\cal O}(\lambda^{4})\,.

(61)

This parametrization preserves the
(I,J)
-interchange symmetry as well as the rescaling invariance property of the separate velocity vectors
\beta_{I}
and
\beta_{J}
. A final comment regarding the parametrization is due in the context of our discussion in section
2
. Recall that there we used
\lambda^{2}
to denote our expansion parameter, while keeping its (leading-power) proportionality coefficient with respect to the squared velocity unspecified, writing

\beta_{K}^{2}=t_{k}\lambda^{2}
(see eq. (
25
)). Here, in eq. (
61
) this relation is specified, and we can therefore extract

t_{k}=\frac{(2\beta_{I}\cdot\beta_{k})(2\beta_{J}\cdot\beta_{k})}{\sqrt{\beta_{I}^{2}\beta_{J}^{2}}}\,.

(62)

4.1
Computation of the one-loop soft anomalous dimension

At one loop, the correlator contains colour dipoles and singlets,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}w^{(1)}=\sum_{I<J}W_{(IJ)}^{(1)}+\cdots,\end{split}

(63)

where here and below ellipsis represent all colour structures that are not relevant for our discussion. Here they simply correspond to kinematic-independent singlet terms, which we ignore (these do not contribute to the computation of
\Omega_{(IJk)}
below). The web
W_{(IJ)}^{(1)}
contains both a colour factor and a kinematic function (see definition in section
3.2
):

\displaystyle W_{(IJ)}^{(1)}=\mathbf{T}_{I}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{J}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}\,.

(64)

The subscript
(IJ)
identifies the web configuration where the Wilson lines
I
and
J
are connected by an exchanged gluon. The kinematic function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}
is expressed in terms of Feynman integrals containing eikonal propagators defined in section
3.1
. Using the Feynman gauge we now write down the kinematic functions for the relevant webs that connects all pairs out of the three lines
I
,
J
and
K
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}=&\,\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IJ.pdf}}}=\,\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}v_{I}\cdot v_{J}\int\left[{\cal D}q\right]Y_{(IJ)}(q)\,,\end{split}

(65a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}=&\,\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JK.pdf}}}=\,\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}v_{J}\cdot v_{K}\int\left[{\cal D}q\right]Y_{(JK)}(q)\,,\end{split}

(65b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IK)}=&\,\,\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IK.pdf}}}=\,\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}v_{I}\cdot v_{K}\int\left[{\cal D}q\right]Y_{(IK)}(q)\,,\end{split}

(65c)

where the normalization factor
{\cal N}
and the one-loop integration measure
\left[{\cal D}q\right]
are defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal N}\equiv\left(\frac{4\pi\mu^{2}}{m^{2}}\right)^{\epsilon}\,,\qquad\quad\left[{\cal D}q\right]\equiv\frac{d^{D}q}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\,,\end{split}

(66)

and the scalar integrand
Y_{(IJ)}
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}Y_{(IJ)}(q)\equiv\frac{1}{P_{g}}\frac{1}{P_{I}}\frac{1}{P_{J}}\,,\end{split}

(67)

with the propagators defined by

P_{g}=q^{2}+i\varepsilon
and

\displaystyle\begin{split}P_{I}=\frac{1}{\tilde{E}_{v_{I}}\left(1,q\right)}\,=\,-v_{I}\cdot q-1+i\varepsilon\,,\qquad P_{J}=\frac{1}{\tilde{E}_{v_{J}}\left(1,-q\right)}\,=\,v_{J}\cdot q-1+i\varepsilon\,,\end{split}

(68)

where we used the rescaled eikonal propagator defined in eq. (
37
), expressed in terms of
v^{\nu}
defined in (
36
) and in terms of the dimensionless momentum vector
q^{\nu}
.
The integrands
Y_{(JK)}
and
Y_{(IK)}
can be obtained from eqs. (
67
) and (
68
) by permutations of the velocities.

Figure 4
:
The propagators and the corresponding parameters for the integral
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}
.

As discussed in section
3.3
, we work in the Euclidean kinematic regime where
\alpha_{IJ}
,
\alpha_{JK}
and
\alpha_{IK}
are all positive. This allows us to reliably identify all regions in the MoR using the geometric algorithm in parametric space. We therefore proceed to write down the Lee-Pomeransky representation for
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}=&\,\,\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}\frac{\Gamma(2-\epsilon)}{\Gamma(1-2\epsilon)}v_{I}\cdot v_{J}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{g}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{I}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{J}\left[{\cal P}_{(IJ)}\right]^{\epsilon-2}.\end{split}

(69)

where the Lee-Pomeransky polynomial
{\cal P}_{(IJ)}
is

\displaystyle{\cal P}_{(IJ)}=\frac{1}{4}x_{I}x_{J}\left(\alpha_{IJ}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)+\frac{1}{4}x_{I}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}x_{J}^{2}+x_{g}x_{I}+x_{g}x_{J}+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}x_{g}}\,,

(70)

where the correspondence between the parameters
x_{i}
and the propagators is defined in figure
4
. The red term (
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}x_{g}}
) in
{\cal P}_{(IJ)}
is the first Symanzik polynomial,
9
9

9

The absence of the parameters
x_{I}
and
x_{J}
in
{\cal U}_{(IJ)}
follows from the absence of quadratic terms in the momenta in the corresponding (eikonal) propagators.

{\cal U}_{(IJ)}
, while the rest of the expression is the second Symanzik polynomial,
{\cal F}_{(IJ)}
. One can confirm that all monomials in the Lee-Pomeransky polynomial (
70
) are non-negative in the chosen kinematic regime, so no hidden regions can arise in the MoR.

Using the package
pySecDec

Heinrich:2023til
;
Borowka:2017idc
, we get the region vectors associated with the expansion in small
\beta_{K}^{2}
. The scaling of the external variables can be found in eqs. (
60
) and (
61
), so we see that the expansion in
\beta_{K}^{2}
corresponds to an expansion in
\alpha_{IK}
and
\alpha_{JK}
, which are both of
{\cal O}(\lambda)
while
\alpha_{IJ}={\cal O}(\lambda^{0})
is unaffected.
As an immediate consequence, one expects that the integral
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}
in eq. (
69
) has only a hard region in the
\lambda
expansion, while those of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IK)}
develop a non-trivial asymptotic expansion.
This is readily confirmed by examining the set of regions vectors
\mathbf{u}^{R}
generated by
pySecDec
for these integrals, which we present in table
1
.
Our notation
for the region vectors of the integral
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}
of eq. (
69
), corresponding to the diagram in figure
4
, is:
\mathbf{u}^{R}=\{u^{R}_{g},u^{R}_{I},u^{R}_{J},1\}
. Following eq. (
55
), this should be read as the following rescaling operation on the integration variables in the Lee-Pomeransky polynomial of eq. (
70
):

(x_{g},x_{I},x_{J})\to(x_{g}\lambda^{u_{g}},x_{I}\lambda^{u_{I}},x_{J}\lambda^{u_{J}})

(71)

:
The region vectors
\mathbf{u}^{R}
and the coefficient
n_{R}
identifying the overall scaling of each region,
\lambda^{n_{R}\epsilon}
(see eq. (
47
)) of the one-loop integrals
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}
.
The regions of the remaining integral
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IK)}
can be obtained by upon replacing
J
by
I
in
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}
.
The rightmost column describes each region in momentum space in terms of the modes defined in table
2
, referring to the scaling of the lightcone momentum components of the gluon.

For
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}
we use the same general convention, applying to
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}
the permutation
I\to J
and
J\to K
simultaneously.
10
10

10

We shall not discuss
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IK)}
explicitly: due to the
(I,J)
symmetry, it can be obtained from the analysis of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}
below by replacing
J
by
I
.

The scaling of the parameters of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}
in the small
\beta_{K}^{2}
limit in a given region
R
should therefore be read as follows:

(x_{g},x_{J},x_{K})\xrightarrow{\;{R}\;}(x_{g}\lambda^{u_{g}^{R}},x_{J}\lambda^{u_{J}^{R}},x_{K}\lambda^{u_{K}^{R}})\,.

(72)

The three region vectors of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}

in table
1(b)

amounts to the rescaling:

\displaystyle(x_{g},x_{J},x_{K})

\displaystyle\,\xrightarrow{\;{\{H\}}\;}(x_{g},x_{J},x_{K}\lambda)\,,

(73a)

\displaystyle(x_{g},x_{J},x_{K})

\displaystyle\,\xrightarrow{\;{\{C_{N}\}}\;}(x_{g},x_{J}\lambda,x_{K})\,,

(73b)

\displaystyle(x_{g},x_{J},x_{K})

\displaystyle\,\xrightarrow{\;{\{C_{\text{IR}}\}}\;}(x_{g}\lambda^{-1},x_{J},x_{K})\,,

(73c)

respectively. The naming convention of the three regions in Table
1(b)
and in eq. (
73
) will be explained below (see table
2
).

Next, we would like to understand the physical interpretation of the three regions.
11
11

11

It is important to stress that the integrals for the separate regions can be easily computed in parameter space (see, appendix
B
below). Our motivation to express the regions in momentum space stems from our interest in their interpretation.

To this end it is useful to revert to momentum space. The basic relation between
the scaling of the parameter
x_{e}
and that of the corresponding propagator
P_{e}
can be read of eq. (
54
), as discussed
12
12

12

We note that the Lee-Pomeransky parameters scale in the same as the Schwinger parameters (see appendix B in
Gardi:2022khw
).
in ref.
Gardi:2022khw
, see eq. (2.45) there:

x_{e}\sim\bar{x}_{e}\sim\frac{1}{P_{e}}\sim\lambda^{u_{e}^{R}}\,.

(74)

So the denominator of the propagator scales
opposite
to the corresponding Lee-Pomeransky parameter. We stress that this applies to the denominator of the propagator as a whole. This of course makes a difference in converting the scaling law from parameter space to momentum space for linear propagators as compared to ordinary quadratic ones.

While the scaling law of the Lee-Pomeransky parameters in a given region
R
is of course Lorentz invariant, the scaling of the loop momentum components is Lorentz frame dependent. We will see that it can nonetheless be determined from the parameter-space region vectors using eq. (
74
), taking into account the full set of scaling laws of the propagators in the diagram, along with momentum conservation.
One additional assumption we will use in making the transition from parameter space to momentum space is that the loop momentum scaling should be consistent with the on-shell condition.
The assumption that facet regions involve only on-shell loop-momentum modes is well motivated in the case of IR regions
Gardi:2022khw
;
Ma:2023hrt
, although it has not been proven.
Our assumption here extends beyond the realm of IR modes; below we state it more precisely.

For what follows let us define the loop momentum in every one-loop diagram as the momentum
q
carried by the gluon. The generalization to two-loop will be discussed in section
4.2
.
We will decompose the momenta
in lightcone coordinates,
q=(q^{+},q^{-},q^{\perp})
, defined by the direction of the external (nearly) lightlike momentum
\beta_{K}\sim(1,\lambda^{2},\lambda)
, such that the velocity in the strict limit is

\beta_{k}=(\beta_{k}^{+},0,0)
.
Next, recall that we have defined the propagators (
68
) using rescaled velocities
v_{J}^{\mu}=\beta_{J}^{\mu}/\sqrt{\beta_{J}^{2}}
according to eq. (
36
). This means that while the timelike Wilson-line velocities admit
v_{J}\sim(1,1,1)
(and similarly
v_{I}\sim(1,1,1)
), for the nearly lightlike Wilson line we have:
v_{K}\sim(\lambda^{-1},\lambda,1)
.

Having fixed our conventions for the parametrization of the loop momentum components
q=(q^{+},q^{-},q^{\perp})
,
we can formulate more precisely the requirement above, that the loop momentum modes should be compatible with the on-shell condition.
If we assume, as in eq. (
46
), that in a given mode in region
R
, the scaling law is

(q^{+},q^{-},q^{\perp})\xrightarrow{\;{R}\;}(q^{+}\lambda^{a_{+}},q^{-}\lambda^{a_{-}},q^{\perp}\lambda^{a_{\perp}})\,,

where
{a_{+}}
,
{a_{-}}
and
{a_{\perp}}
are three rational numbers, then compatibility with the on-shell condition simply implies

{a_{+}}+{a_{-}}=2{a_{\perp}},

(75)

so that an inverse propagator,

q^{2}=2q^{+}q^{-}-|p_{\perp}|^{2}\xrightarrow{\;{R}\;}q^{2}\lambda^{2{a_{\perp}}}

admits
homogeneous scaling
between the product of lightcone components and squared transverse components. Clearly, eq. (
75
) is violated for potential or Glauber modes, which are associated with hidden regions in parameter space
Jantzen:2012mw
;
Gardi:2024axt
.
Recall that in the context of the present work we are working in the Euclidean regime, and hence we only have facet regions. In this context we will be assuming that the condition of eq. (
75
) holds for every loop momentum mode (including UV modes!) and will provide further evidence for the validity of this assumption through the region analysis at one and two loops.

Let us now apply the relation of eq. (
74
) to infer the scaling of the propagators corresponding to each of the three regions
in eq. (
73
):

\displaystyle\left[q^{2},\quad-v_{J}\cdot q-1,\quad v_{K}\cdot q-1\right]

\displaystyle\,\xrightarrow{\;{\{H\}}\;}\left[q^{2},\quad-v_{J}\cdot q-1,\quad(v_{K}\cdot q-1)\lambda^{-1}\right]\,,

(76a)

\displaystyle\left[q^{2},\quad-v_{J}\cdot q-1,\quad v_{K}\cdot q-1\right]

\displaystyle\,\xrightarrow{\;{\{C_{N}\}}\;}\left[q^{2},\quad(-v_{J}\cdot q-1)\lambda^{-1},\quad v_{K}\cdot q-1\right]\,,

(76b)

\displaystyle\left[q^{2},\quad-v_{J}\cdot q-1,\quad v_{K}\cdot q-1\right]

\displaystyle\,\xrightarrow{\;{\{C_{\text{IR}}\}}\;}\left[q^{2}\lambda,\quad-v_{J}\cdot q-1,\quad v_{K}\cdot q-1\right]\,.

(76c)

Next, let us interpret these scaling laws by inferring the scaling of the separate lightcone momentum components. To this end we need to impose that
v_{J}\sim(1,1,1)
and
v_{K}\sim(\lambda^{-1},\lambda,1)
.

Let us begin by considering (
76a
) and (
76b
). We notice that in both of these regions, the gluon propagator remain
{\cal O}(\lambda^{0})
, while in each one of the eikonal propagators scales by
\lambda^{-1}
.
Let us expand the propagators in lightcone coordinates. They are given respectively by

\displaystyle q^{2}

\displaystyle\,=2q^{+}q^{-}-|q^{\perp}|^{2}\,,

(77a)

\displaystyle v_{J}\cdot q-1

\displaystyle\,=(v_{J}^{+},v_{J}^{-},v_{J}^{\perp})\cdot(q^{+},q^{-},q^{\perp})-1=v_{J}^{+}q^{-}+v_{J}^{-}q^{+}-v_{J}^{\perp}\cdot q^{\perp}-1\,,

(77b)

\displaystyle v_{K}\cdot q-1

\displaystyle\,=(v_{K}^{+},v_{K}^{-},v_{K}^{\perp})\cdot(q^{+},q^{-},q^{\perp})-1=v_{K}^{+}q^{-}+v_{K}^{-}q^{+}-v_{K}^{\perp}\cdot q^{\perp}-1\,.

(77c)

Since
v_{J}\sim(\lambda^{0},\lambda^{0},\lambda^{0})
and
v_{K}\sim(\lambda^{-1},\lambda,\lambda^{0})

we see that the region of
eq. (
76a
)
corresponds to
(q^{+},q^{-},q^{\perp})\sim(\lambda^{0},\lambda^{0},\lambda^{0})

while that of eq. (
76b
) to

(q^{+},q^{-},q^{\perp})\sim(\lambda^{-1},\lambda,\lambda^{0})
.

The final region to interpret in momentum space is (
76c
). Here we see that the gluon virtulaity scales as
\lambda^{1}
, while the two eikonal propagators remain
\sim\lambda^{0}
. An on-shell mode consistent with this is

(q^{+},q^{-},q^{\perp})\sim(1,\lambda^{1},\sqrt{\lambda})
, which we call an IR collinear mode.

The three loop-momentum modes defining these three regions are summarized in the first three rows of table
2
. We notice that while the hard (
H
) and IR collinear (
C_{\text{IR}}
) modes are commonplace in the on-shell expansion of wide-angle scattering non-eikonal integrals
Gardi:2022khw
;
Ma:2023hrt
, the mode
C_{N}
is rather special, as it involves both large and small momentum components. It is a feature of the eikonal approximation. We refer to it as the
neutral
collinear mode. This mode presents a potential ambiguity in disentangling IR and UV singularities, and we will discuss it further in section
5
.

With the region information given in table
1
, it is straightforward to write down the asymptotic expansion for kinematic functions,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}\right]=&\sum_{R}\lambda^{n_{R}\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}^{R}=\,\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IJH.pdf}}},\end{split}

(78a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IK)}\right]=&\sum_{R}\lambda^{n_{R}\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IK)}^{R}=\,\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{H\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{N}\}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}\\
=&\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IKH.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IKCN.pdf}}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IKCIR.pdf}}},\end{split}

(78b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}\right]=&\sum_{R}\lambda^{n_{R}\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)}^{R}=\,\,{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{N}\}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}\\
=&\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JKH.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JKHCN.pdf}}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JKHCIR.pdf}}}.\end{split}

(78c)

All three regions contribute at leading power in
\lambda
.

Table 2
:
Loop momentum modes in the region analysis in terms of lightcone coordinates
(q^{+},q^{-},q^{\perp})
, where the external (nearly) lightlike line
K
has velocity
\beta_{K}\sim(1,\lambda^{2},\lambda)
, or equivalently, normalized velocity
v_{K}\sim(\lambda^{-1},\lambda,1)
. Out of the five modes listed here, only the first three – IR and neutral modes – appear in one-loop integrals, see table
1
, while the remaining two – UV modes – appear first at two loops, see tables
3
and
4
below.

By definition, the leading power of the hard region corresponds to the computation of the correlator with a strictly lightlike Wilson line defined by eq. (
38
). We therefore single out the hard region by splitting the asymptotic expansion of the correlator (at any given loop order
n
) into two parts,

{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[w^{(n)}\right]=h^{(n)}+r^{(n)},

(79)

where
h^{(n)}
contains the hard region only, while
r^{(n)}
contains all the remaining regions. The contributions to
h^{(n)}
and
r^{(n)}
can be further decomposed by colour.
In
r^{(n)}
each colour structure is multiplied by an asymptotic expansion consisting of
region functions
, as in eq. (
59
). Each such region function may in general receive contributions from different webs, i.e. different
{\mathcal{Y}}_{ij}^{R}
.

At one loop,
h^{(1)}
and
r^{(1)}
may be written in terms of the region functions
{\cal R}
as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}h^{(1)}=\,\,&\sum_{I<J}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}h^{(1)}_{IJ}+\sum_{I}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}h^{(1)}_{Ik}+\cdots\\
=\,\,&\sum_{I<J}\mathbf{T}_{I}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{J}{\cal R}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}+\sum_{I}\mathbf{T}_{I}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{k}{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{H\}}+\cdots,\end{split}

(80a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}r^{(1)}=\,\,&\sum_{I}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}r^{(1)}_{(Ik)}+\cdots\\
=\,\,&\sum_{I}\mathbf{T}_{I}\cdot\mathbf{T}_{k}\left\{{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{N}\}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}\right\}+\cdots.\end{split}

(80b)

Note that only the colour structures involving the lightlike line
k
will enter
r^{(1)}
, because others will not be influenced by the expansion, so they are fully contained in
h^{(1)}
. While in the multi-loop context the region functions
{\cal R}^{R}
with a given colour structure are combinations of several kinematic functions
{\mathcal{Y}}_{ij}^{R}
, at one loop, there is just one web (in fact, one diagram), hence one function
{\mathcal{Y}}^{R}
, contributing to each region function
{\cal R}^{R}
, as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}\,&={\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IJH.pdf}}},\qquad{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{H\}}={\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{H\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IkH.pdf}}},\qquad{\cal R}_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}={\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JkH.pdf}}},\\
{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{N}\}}&\,={\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{N}\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IKCN.pdf}}},\qquad{\cal R}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{N}\}}={\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{N}\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JKHCN.pdf}}},\\
{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}&\,={\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IKCIR.pdf}}},\qquad{\cal R}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}={\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JKHCIR.pdf}}}.\end{split}

(81)

The invariant functions
\mathbb{R}^{(n_{R})}
are then simply the sum of the region functions
{\cal R}^{R}
having a common
n_{R}
, namely

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{R}_{(IJ)}^{(0)}=\,\,&{\cal R}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}\,,\\
\mathbb{R}_{(JK)}^{(0)}=\,\,&{\cal R}_{(JK)}^{\{H\}}+{\cal R}_{(JK)}^{\{C_{N}\}}\,,\qquad\mathbb{R}_{(JK)}^{(-1)}={\cal R}_{(JK)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}\,,\\
\mathbb{R}_{(IK)}^{(0)}=\,\,&{\cal R}_{(IK)}^{\{H\}}+{\cal R}_{(IK)}^{\{C_{N}\}}\,,\qquad\mathbb{R}_{(IK)}^{(-1)}={\cal R}_{(IK)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}\,.\end{split}

(82)

The one-loop region functions at leading order in
\lambda
and through finite terms in
\epsilon
are summarized below. Detailed computations can be found in appendix
B
, where explicit results are provided through
{\cal O}(\epsilon^{1})
. Keeping the
{\cal O}(\epsilon^{1})
contribution at one loop is necessary, since they contribute to the renormalization of the two-loop functions.

We note that all three regions contributing to the
(Jk)
web (and similarly to the
(Ik)
web), namely the hard, the neutral collinear and the IR collinear regions, have double poles in
\epsilon
. We shall see that these cancel in the sum of regions, as they must do to reproduce the expansion of the fully-timelike web
(JK)
.

It is also interesting to note that the neutral region functions,
{\cal R}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{N}\}}
and
{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{N}\}}
, are independent with any kinematic variables; see eq. (
219
). This observation will become important in section
5
below, where we will discuss the IR versus UV origin of the various contributions, referring to the special role of the neutral collinear mode.

The contributions of the hard region to the one-loop correlator with a strictly lightlike like
k
and two timelike lines
I
and
J
is:

\displaystyle\begin{split}h^{(1)}_{(IJ)}=\,\,&\textbf{T}_{I}\cdot\textbf{T}_{J}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\bigg[-\frac{2}{\epsilon}\log(\alpha_{IJ})+V_{1}(\alpha_{IJ})+{\cal O}(\epsilon)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\bigg],\end{split}

(83a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}h^{(1)}_{(Ik)}=\,\,&\textbf{T}_{I}\cdot\textbf{T}_{k}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}-\frac{5\pi^{2}}{12}+{\cal O}(\epsilon)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\right],\end{split}

(83b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}h^{(1)}_{(Jk)}=\,\,&\textbf{T}_{J}\cdot\textbf{T}_{k}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}-\frac{5\pi^{2}}{12}+{\cal O}(\epsilon)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\right],\end{split}

(83c)

where

V_{1}(\alpha)=-4\text{Li}_{2}(-\alpha)+\log^{2}(\alpha)-4\log(\alpha+1)\log(\alpha)-\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}
(an equivalent expression in terms of Goncharov polylogs is provided in appendix
A
). The scale
\bar{m}
is defined as

\displaystyle\bar{m}\equiv m\sqrt{\frac{e^{\gamma_{\text{E}}}}{\pi}}\,.

(84)

Similarly, the contributions of the remaining (non-hard) regions to the one-loop correlator with a lightlike like
k
and two timelike lines
I
and
J
are

\displaystyle\begin{split}r^{(1)}_{(Ik)}=\,\,&\textbf{T}_{I}\cdot\textbf{T}_{k}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{y_{IJk}}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\left[\log^{2}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{y_{IJk}}\right)+\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}\right]+{\cal O}(\epsilon)\\
&\,\hskip 100.0pt+{\cal O}(\lambda)\bigg\},\end{split}

(85a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}r^{(1)}_{(Jk)}=\,\,&\textbf{T}_{J}\cdot\textbf{T}_{k}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}-\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\left(\lambda^{2}y_{IJk}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\left[\log^{2}\left(\lambda^{2}y_{IJk}\right)+\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}\right]+{\cal O}(\epsilon)\\
&\,\hskip 100.0pt+{\cal O}(\lambda)\bigg\},\end{split}

(85b)

As one expects, the IR region contributions include logarithms of the expasnion parameter. In the way we performed the asymptotic expansion (see eq. (
60
)), the variables
\frac{\lambda^{2}}{y_{IJk}}
and
\lambda^{2}y_{IJk}
in eqs. (
85a
) and (
85b
) are, respectively,
\alpha_{IK}^{2}
and
\alpha_{JK}^{2}
, which are considered infinitesimally small in the limit.

Next we note that all double poles in
\epsilon
cancel, for each colour structure, between the hard region,
h^{(1)}
, and the other regions,
r^{(1)}
.
Upon summing up all the regions, we get the correlator in the asymptotic expansion,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[w^{(1)}\right]=\,\,&\sum_{I<J}\textbf{T}_{I}\cdot\textbf{T}_{J}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\bigg[-\frac{2}{\epsilon}\log(\alpha_{IJ})+V_{1}(\alpha_{IJ})+{\cal O}(\epsilon)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\bigg]\\
&+\sum_{I}\textbf{T}_{I}\cdot\textbf{T}_{k}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log\left(\frac{(2\beta_{I}\cdot\beta_{k})^{2}}{t_{k}\lambda^{2}\beta_{I}^{2}}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}\left(\frac{(2\beta_{I}\cdot\beta_{k})^{2}}{t_{k}\lambda^{2}\beta_{I}^{2}}\right)-\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}+{\cal O}(\epsilon)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\bigg\}\\
&+\cdots\,,\end{split}

(86)

where we have restored the coefficient
t_{k}
of eq. (
62
).

To obtain the soft AD, we write explicitly the terms in the Laurent expansion in
\epsilon
for each order of
h^{(n)}
and
r^{(n)}
as follows,

\displaystyle h^{(n)}=\,\,

\displaystyle\sum_{l}h^{(n,l)}\epsilon^{l}=\sing\left({h}^{(n)}\right)+\reg\left({h}^{(n)}\right),

(87a)

\displaystyle r^{(n)}=\,\,

\displaystyle\sum_{l}r^{(n,l)}\epsilon^{l}=\sing\left({r}^{(n)}\right)+\reg\left({r}^{(n)}\right),

(87b)

where we defined the singular (sometimes called principal part) and regular part operators acting on the Laurent expansion of a function
f(\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\sing(f(\epsilon))=\sum_{l\leq-1}f^{(l)}\epsilon^{l},\qquad\reg(f(\epsilon))=\sum_{l\geq 0}f^{(l)}\epsilon^{l}\,,

(88)

and
\sing(f(\epsilon))+\reg(f(\epsilon))=f(\epsilon)
.
Finally, using eq. (
22b
), the soft ADs are given by,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\Gamma_{\mathrm{UV}}^{(1)}=\frac{1}{\epsilon}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[-2w^{(1,-1)}\right]=\,\,&-\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}\left(h^{(1,-2)}+r^{(1,-2)}\right)-\frac{2}{\epsilon}\left(h^{(1,-1)}+r^{(1,-1)}\right)\\
=\,\,&-2\sing\left(h^{(1)}+r^{(1)}\right)\,.\end{split}

(89)

Upon substituting in the functions, the double pole vanishes owing the an exact cancellation between the three regions, and the soft AD of eq. (
27a
) is exactly reproduced.

4.2
Computation of the two-loop two-mass tripole

Starting from two loops, colour tripoles contribute to correlators of Wilson lines, and hence to the soft AD, provided that at least two of the particles are massive. In what follows we compute the two-loop correlator of three Wilson lines in the limit where one of them becomes lightlike. We will use the MoR, and follow the same steps we took above at one loop, focusing now exclusively to the tripoles contributions
\mathbf{T}_{IJK}
in the limit
\beta_{K}^{2}\to 0
.

The correlator
of a product of any number of timelike Wilson lines
at two loops takes the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}w^{(2)}=\,\,&\sum_{I<J<K}\left[W_{(IJK)}^{(2)}+W_{(IJ)(IK)}^{(2)}+W_{(JK)(JI)}^{(2)}+W_{(KI)(KJ)}^{(2)}\right]+\cdots\\
=\,\,&\sum_{I<J<K}\mathbf{T}_{IJK}\,\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}^{(2)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{(2)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{(2)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{(2)}\right]+\cdots,\end{split}

(90)

where we identified the four webs that contribute with the colour structure
\mathbf{T}_{(IJK)}
, and where
{\mathcal{Y}}
represent the corresponding kinematically dependent function, as discussed in section
3.2
. The most complicated of these is
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}
, where the three Wilson lines
I
,
J
and
K
are connected by a three-gluon vertex.

4.2.1
The fully connected two-loop tripole web

The fully-connected tripole web was computed in refs.
Ferroglia:2009ii
;
Ferroglia:2009ep
(see also
Mitov:2009sv
) for the general case of timelike Wilson lines, as part of the computation of the soft AD quoted in eq. (
15
) above. Here we proceed to compute it using the MoR, expanding in
\beta_{K}^{2}
.

The kinematic function of this fully-connected web is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}=&\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111IJK.pdf}}}=\,\,\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}v_{I}^{\mu}v_{J}^{\nu}v_{K}^{\rho}\int\left[{\cal D}q\right]^{2}L_{\mu\nu\rho}(q_{I},q_{J},q_{K})\times Y_{(IJK)}(q_{I},q_{J},q_{K}),\end{split}

(91)

where the two-loop measure
\left[{\cal D}q\right]^{2}
and the numerator
L_{\mu\nu\rho}
related to the three-gluon vertex are

\displaystyle\left[{\cal D}q\right]^{2}\equiv

\displaystyle\,\,\left(\frac{1}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\right)^{2}\,d^{D}q_{I}\,d^{D}q_{J}\,d^{D}q_{K}\,\delta^{D}(q_{I}+q_{J}+q_{K}),

(92)

\displaystyle L_{\mu\nu\rho}(q_{I},q_{J},q_{K})\equiv

\displaystyle\,\,g_{\mu\nu}\left(q_{I}-q_{J}\right)_{\rho}+g_{\nu\rho}\left(q_{J}-q_{K}\right)_{\mu}+g_{\rho\mu}\left(q_{K}-q_{I}\right)_{\nu}.

(93)

The scalar integrand
Y_{(IJK)}
is defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}Y_{(IJK)}(k_{I},k_{J},k_{K})\equiv&\,\,\frac{1}{P_{g_{I}}}\frac{1}{P_{g_{J}}}\frac{1}{P_{g_{K}}}\frac{1}{P_{I}}\frac{1}{P_{J}}\frac{1}{P_{K}},\end{split}

(94)

with the six propagators:

\displaystyle\begin{split}P_{g_{I}}=&\,\,q_{I}^{2}+i\varepsilon\,,\qquad P_{I}=\frac{1}{\tilde{E}_{v_{I}}(1,q_{I})}=-v_{I}\cdot q_{I}-1+i\varepsilon\,,\\
P_{g_{J}}=&\,\,q_{J}^{2}+i\varepsilon\,,\qquad P_{J}=\frac{1}{\tilde{E}_{v_{J}}(1,q_{J})}=-v_{J}\cdot q_{J}-1+i\varepsilon\,,\\
P_{g_{K}}=&\,\,q_{K}^{2}+i\varepsilon\,,\qquad P_{K}=\frac{1}{\tilde{E}_{v_{K}}(1,q_{K})}=-v_{K}\cdot q_{K}-1+i\varepsilon\,.\end{split}

(95)

From these definitions, it is straightforward to check that the scalar integrand
Y_{(IJK)}
is symmetric under the interchange of any pair of the of external lines
I
,
J
and
K
, while the numerator
v_{I}^{\mu}v_{J}^{\nu}v_{K}^{\rho}L_{\mu\nu\rho}(q_{I},q_{J},q_{K})
is totally antisymmetric with respect to such an interchange.
Thus, the kinematic function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}
is consistent with the antisymmetry of the colour factor
\mathbf{T}_{(IJK)}
, and the Bose-symmetric nature of the web
W_{(IJK)}
as a whole.

Because the numerator
L_{\mu\nu\rho}(q_{I},q_{J},q_{K})
depends on loop momenta, the kinematic function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}
is a combination of several scalar integrals, written in Lee-Pomeransky representation. However, the full set of regions is determined by the denominator, while the numerator only suppresses or enhances certain regions. To figure out the region structures, we analyze the following scalar integral which contains all propagators in eq. (
95
) and is represented by a single Lee-Pomeransky integrand

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJK)}\equiv\,\,\int\left[{\cal D}q\right]^{2}Y_{(IJK)}(q_{I},q_{J},q_{K}).

(96)

The Lee-Pomeransky representation of this integral is

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJK)}=\frac{\Gamma(2-\epsilon)}{\Gamma(-3\epsilon)}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{g_{I}}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{g_{J}}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{g_{K}}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{I}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{J}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{K}\left[{\cal P}_{(IJK)}\right]^{\epsilon-2}

(97)

with its polynomial
{\cal P}_{(IJK)}
given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal P}_{(IJK)}=\,&\frac{x_{g_{K}}}{4}x_{J}x_{I}\left(\alpha_{IJ}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)+\frac{x_{g_{J}}}{4}x_{K}x_{I}\left(\alpha_{IK}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IK}}\right)+\frac{x_{g_{I}}}{4}x_{J}x_{K}\left(\alpha_{JK}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{JK}}\right)\\
&+\frac{x_{g_{K}}}{4}\left(x_{I}^{2}+x_{J}^{2}\right)+\frac{x_{g_{J}}}{4}\left(x_{I}^{2}+x_{K}^{2}\right)+\frac{x_{g_{I}}}{4}\left(x_{J}^{2}+x_{K}^{2}\right)\\
&+\left(x_{g_{J}}x_{g_{K}}+x_{g_{I}}x_{g_{J}}+x_{g_{I}}x_{g_{K}}\right)\left(x_{I}+x_{J}+x_{K}\right)+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}x_{g_{I}}x_{g_{J}}+x_{g_{K}}x_{g_{J}}+x_{g_{I}}x_{g_{K}}}\,.\end{split}

(98)

The correspondence between the parameters and the propagators is displayed in figure
5
. The red monomials in eq. (
98
) originate in the first Synamzik polynomial,
{\cal U}_{(IJK)}
. These monomials only involve the parameters associated with gluon propagators, as expected.

The region vectors of the
small
\beta_{K}^{2}
expansion of the scalar integral
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}
of eq. (
96
), and hence of the original connected web
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}
of eq. (
91
), computed using
pySecDec
, are collected in table
3
. The components of the region vector
\mathbf{u}^{R}
follow the order,

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}^{R}=\{u_{g_{I}}^{R},u_{g_{J}}^{R},u_{g_{K}}^{R},u_{I}^{R},u_{J}^{R},u_{K}^{R},1\}\,.

(99)

This vector defines the scaling law or the Lee-Pomeransky parameters in region
R
as follows,

\displaystyle\{x_{g_{I}},x_{g_{J}},x_{g_{K}},x_{I},x_{J},x_{K}\}\xrightarrow{\;{R}\;}\{x_{g_{I}}\lambda^{u_{g_{I}}^{R}},x_{g_{J}}\lambda^{u_{g_{J}}^{R}},x_{g_{K}}\lambda^{u_{g_{K}}^{R}},x_{I}\lambda^{u_{I}^{R}},x_{J}\lambda^{u_{J}^{R}},x_{K}\lambda^{u_{K}^{R}}\}\,.

(100)

We note that these regions consist of five modes, which are summarized in table
2
. Two of the modes appear first at two loops, while the remaining three have already been encountered at one loop (table
1
).

In appendices
C
,
D
and
E
, we provide some details regarding the computation and the result of each region, after expansion to leading order in
\lambda
. The most complicated integral is the one corresponding to the hard region. To evaluate this integral we used the method of differential equations, which is setup in appendix
D.3.1
. The remaining region integrals were performed directly in parameter space.

Figure 5
:

\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJK)}
.

Table 3
:

Summary of the region analysis of
the two-loop connected web integral
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJK)}
. The second column shows the
region vectors
\mathbf{u}^{R}
; the third displays the coefficient
n_{R}
identifying the overall scaling of each region,
\lambda^{n_{R}\epsilon}
as a sum of the
n_{R}
contributions of the two modes (see eq. (
48
)); the fourth column displays the loop modes associated with two of the three gluon propagators in figure
5
, defining the region in momentum space, as explained in the text. See Table
2
for the definition of each mode.

Figure 6
:
The diagram corresponding to region
[H,*,C_{N}]
. The two-fold line
represents simply the addition (owing to momentum conservation,
q_{J}=-q_{I}-q_{K}
) of the momentum components of the hard mode
H
and the neutral collinear mode
C_{N}
. The scaling of such a gluon is
(\lambda^{-1},1,1)
in lightcone coordinates.

Before presenting the result, we would like to discuss the interpretation of the regions in momentum space, along the lines of the one-loop analysis in section
4.1
. Considering the momentum representation in eq. (
91
) we note that the Dirac
\delta
function in eq. (
92
) allows us to readily integrate any one of three momenta
\{q_{I},q_{J},q_{K}\}
, thus choosing the remaining two to serve as the two independent loop momenta, where the one we integrated over is fixed by momentum conservation at the three-gluon vertex. However, in contrast to the integral before the region expansion, the choice of the two independent loop momenta in a given region matters. The principle in choosing the two independent momenta is that they should provide the complete information regarding the modes. We therefore introduce suitable notation to encode which two loop momenta are chosen to describe the modes in each region in the form of ordered brackets with three slots, corresponding to the three gluon propagators, following the order
\left[\text{mode}_{q_{I}},\text{mode}_{q_{J}},\text{mode}_{q_{K}}\right]
.
The entry with an asterisk identifies the propagator whose momentum is determined using momentum conservation.
This notation is used in
table
3
, which lists all the regions of
the (scalar) kinematic function is then
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left[\text{mode}_{q_{I}},\text{mode}_{q_{J}},\text{mode}_{q_{K}}\right]}
, where one of the modes is replaced by an asterisk.

As an example, consider the region vector
\{0,1,0,0,1,0,1\}
in table
3
, which corresponds to the region
[H,*,C_{N}]
shown in figure
6
. The scaling law of the two independent loop momenta in this region is

\displaystyle q_{I}\sim(1,1,1)\,\,,\qquad q_{K}\sim(\lambda^{-1},\lambda,1)\,.

(101)

while

\displaystyle q_{J}=-q_{I}-q_{K}\sim(\lambda^{-1},1,1)

(102)

is determined from by momentum conservation.
Note that if one attempt to describe this region instead by regarding
q_{J}
and
q_{K}
as the two independent loop momenta, then

q_{I}
can be consistent with the hard mode scaling law, only if there is a finely-tuned cancellation between the positive components of the two momenta,

\displaystyle q_{I}^{+}=-q_{J}^{+}-q_{K}^{+}\sim O(\lambda^{-1})-O(\lambda^{-1})\sim O(\lambda^{0}).

(103)

Therefore, eq. (
103
) should be understood as an extra condition in addition the scaling law of
q_{J}
and
q_{K}
. A similar situation will happen if we keep
q_{I}
and
q_{J}
. As a result, the only faithful description of the region purely in terms of a scaling law is according to eq. (
101
).

Before we return to the asymptotic expansion of the original kinematic function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}
, we define, as an intermediate step, functions which sum the region integrals with the same type of modes, and thus the same
n_{R}
. Since
n_{R}
corresponds to the sum of the respective
n_{R}
values for each of the two independent loop momenta, as shown in general in eq. (
48
) (and implemented in the third column of table
3
), the total value of
n_{R}
is the same independently of the association of modes with specific propagators, for instance, the two regions
{[H,*,C_{N}]}
and
{[*,H,C_{N}]}
have the same total
n_{R}
. We therefore introduce a notation for the unordered set of modes using curly brackets, for instance
\{H,C_{N}\}
in the example above.

We now collect the regions into three classes according to eq. (
3.3
):
IR regions, given by the first four rows in table
3
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}\equiv\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}},*]}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CIRCIR.pdf}}}\,,\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}\equiv\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[H,*,C_{\mathrm{IR}}]}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CIRH.pdf}}}\,,\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}\equiv\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{N},*]}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}},*]}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CNCIRa.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CNCIRb.pdf}}}\,,\end{split}

(104)

neutral regions, given by the subsequent four rows (rows 5-8) in table
3
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}\equiv\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[H,H,*]}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111HH.pdf}}}\,,\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},C_{N}\}}\equiv\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[C_{N},C_{N},*]}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CNCN.pdf}}}\,,\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H\}}\equiv\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[H,*,C_{N}]}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[*,H,C_{N}]}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111HCNap.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111HCNbp.pdf}}}\,,\end{split}

(105)

and UV regions, given by the bottom three rows (rows 9-11) in table
3
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\}}\equiv\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[H_{\mathrm{UV}},*,C_{N}]}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111HUVCN.pdf}}}\,,\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\}}\equiv\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[H,*,C_{\mathrm{UV}}]}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{[*,H,C_{\mathrm{UV}}]}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CUVHb.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CUVHa.pdf}}}\,.\end{split}

(106)

Importantly, we observe that there exists no region which involves one loop in an IR mode with another loop in a UV mode. Only mixing within these two classes, and between each of them and neutral modes, appear as regions. This property will be useful in unambiguously associating the region contribution to the UV or IR renormalization in what follows.

The results of the IR, neutral and UV regions, can be found in the appendices
C
,

D
and
E
, respectively.
The IR regions,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}
, and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}
are given in eqs. (
246
), (
232
) and (
255
), respectively. We note that these integrals yield triple and double poles in
\epsilon
in addition to the single pole the correlator is expected to have.

The two neutral regions,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},C_{N}\}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H\}}
, are zero,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},C_{N}\}}={\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H\}}=0\,.

(107)

The reason why they are trivial will be elucidated in section
4.3
.
The strict limit of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}
, which is the hard region
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}
, gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg[-\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}U_{1}(y_{IJk})-2\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\log(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg]\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg(2V_{1}(\alpha_{IJ})+M_{100}(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}U_{2}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\\
&+2\log^{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)-\frac{2}{3}\log^{3}\left(y_{IJk}\right)-\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(108)

where the transcendental functions
V_{1}
,
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
can be found in appendix
A
.
We note that the leading order of the hard region features a double pole in
\epsilon
, which is a mix of UV and collinear (IR) singularities.

The UV regions
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\}}
are given in eq. (
295
) and eq. (
302
), respectively. These are new at two loops and their interpretation will be discussed in the next section.
Similarly to the IR regions, also the UV ones feature up to cubic poles in
\epsilon
.

According to the method of regions, and in line with eq. (
58
), the expanded result for the web
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}
in the small
\beta_{K}^{2}
limit is given by the sum of all the regions in eqs. (
104
), (
105
) and (
106
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}\right]=\,\,&\lambda^{-2\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}\\[4.26773pt]
&+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}}\\[4.26773pt]
&+\lambda^{2\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,C_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}+\lambda^{2\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}\,,\end{split}

(109)

where the vanishing regions are marked in blue. The IR, neutral and UV regions, respectively, are arranged in the three lines.
The first line sums all IR regions, where
n_{R}=-1
in the case of a single
C_{\text{IR}}
loop-momentum mode
or
-2
for two such independent modes. This sum features a triple pole in
\epsilon
as the leading behavior. The second line in eq. (
109
), consists of the neutral regions with
n_{R}=0
. These involve the combination of two neutral loop-momentum modes, either hard or neutral-collinear, and are a natural generalization of the regions we have observed at one loop (see table
1
). However, since the latter two vanish, the sum of all neutral regions is exactly eq. (
108
). Finally, the third line in eq. (
109
),
consists of the two UV regions, for which
n_{R}=2
. Similarly to the case of the IR regions, the sum of the UV regions also starts with a triple pole.

By summing up all the regions, the triple pole in
\epsilon
from the UV and IR regions cancel each other out. In addition, the double poles present in the separate IR, neutral and UV components cancel entirely in the sum.
Furthermore, the polylogarithmic functions entering the coefficient of the single
\epsilon
pole via
U_{1}
,
U_{2}
,
V_{1}
and
M_{100}
, also
cancel between the hard region and the IR and UV regions.
As a result, the leading order of the expansion
{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}\right]
is – as expected – a single pole in
\epsilon
times a sum of products of logarithms,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}\right]=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg[-4\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(\alpha_{IJ})\log(\lambda)\\
&+2\log^{2}(\alpha_{IJ})+2\log^{2}(\lambda)-\frac{1}{2}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})\bigg]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda^{1}).\end{split}

(110)

Notice that eq. (
110
) still contains
\log(\lambda)
, indicating that the limit
\beta_{K}^{2}\to 0
of this web (on its own) is singular.
One can check that upon substituting
\lambda
in terms of
\beta_{K}^{2}
according to eq. (
61
), then eq. (
110
) exactly matches the expansion of this web computed with three timelike Wilson lines
Mitov:2009sv
;
Ferroglia:2009ii
;
Ferroglia:2009ep
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{2}{\epsilon}\epsilon^{IJK}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(\alpha_{IJ})\log^{2}(\alpha_{IK})+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\,,\end{split}

(111)

where
\epsilon^{IJK}
is the Levi-Civita tensor. Again we have verified that the MoR correctly reproduces the asymptotic expansion.

4.2.2
Tripole contributions from multiple gluon exchange webs

The remaining two-loop webs in eq. (
90
) are not fully connected, but consist of two separate gluon exchanges between the three Wilson lines.
There are three different web configurations of this kind,
W_{(IJ)(IK)}
,
W_{(JK)(JI)}
and
W_{(KI)(KJ)}
, which are related by permutations of external lines.
Beyond computation of the soft AD
Mitov:2009sv
;
Ferroglia:2009ii
;
Ferroglia:2009ep
, this type of web has been studied in detail in refs.
Gardi:2013saa
;
Falcioni:2014pka
for generic timelike Wilson lines, where it was used as the simplest example of multiple gluon exchange webs going beyond the cusp configuration. Here we consider its asymptotic expansion at small
\beta_{K}^{2}
, pursing the same analysis as for the one-loop and the connected tripole webs above.

Each of the
W_{(IJ)(IK)}
,
W_{(JK)(JI)}
and
W_{(KI)(KJ)}
webs has a kinematic function which is a difference of two integrals,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}=&\frac{1}{2}\left\{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211IJKa.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211IJKb.pdf}}}\right\}\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\frac{{\cal N}^{2}}{2}v_{J}\cdot v_{I}v_{K}\cdot v_{I}\int\left[{\cal D}q_{J}\right]\left[{\cal D}q_{K}\right]\left[Y_{(IJ)(IK)}(q_{J},q_{K})-Y_{(IK)(IJ)}(q_{K},q_{J})\right],\end{split}

(112a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}=&\frac{1}{2}\left\{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121IJKb.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121IJKa.pdf}}}\right\}\\
=&\,\,\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\frac{{\cal N}^{2}}{2}v_{I}\cdot v_{J}v_{K}\cdot v_{J}\int\left[{\cal D}q_{I}\right]\left[{\cal D}q_{K}\right]\left[Y_{(JK)(JI)}(q_{K},q_{I})-Y_{(JI)(JK)}(q_{I},q_{K})\right],\end{split}

(112b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}=&\frac{1}{2}\left\{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112IJKa.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112IJKb.pdf}}}\right\}\\
=&\,\,\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\frac{{\cal N}^{2}}{2}v_{I}\cdot v_{K}v_{J}\cdot v_{K}\int\left[{\cal D}q_{J}\right]\left[{\cal D}q_{I}\right]\left[Y_{(KI)(KJ)}(q_{I},q_{J})-Y_{(KJ)(KI)}(q_{J},q_{I})\right],\end{split}

(112c)

where
{\cal N}
and the measure
\left[{\cal D}q\right]
are defined in eq. (
66
), and the scalar integrand
Y_{(IJ)(IK)}
is defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}Y_{(IJ)(IK)}(k_{J},k_{K})\equiv&\,\,\frac{1}{P_{g_{J}}}\frac{1}{P_{g_{K}}}\frac{1}{P_{J}}\frac{1}{P_{K}}\frac{1}{P_{I,J}}\frac{1}{P_{I,JK}}\,,\end{split}

(113)

with the six propagators given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}P_{g_{J}}=\,\,&q_{J}^{2}+i\varepsilon\,,\qquad P_{J}=\frac{1}{\tilde{E}_{\beta_{J}}\left(1,q_{I}\right)}=-v_{J}\cdot q_{J}-1+i\varepsilon\,,\\
P_{g_{K}}=\,\,&q_{K}^{2}+i\varepsilon\,,\qquad P_{K}=\frac{1}{\tilde{E}_{\beta_{K}}\left(1,q_{K}\right)}=-v_{K}\cdot q_{K}-1+i\varepsilon\\
P_{I,J}=\,\,&\frac{1}{\tilde{E}_{\beta_{I}}\left(1,-q_{J}\right)}=v_{I}\cdot q_{J}-1+i\varepsilon\,,\\
P_{I,JK}=\,\,&\frac{1}{\tilde{E}_{\beta_{I}}\left(2,-q_{J}-q_{K}\right)}=v_{I}\cdot(q_{J}+q_{K})-2+i\varepsilon\,.\end{split}

(114)

With the definitions in eq. (
112
), the kinematic function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
is by construction anti-symmetric on the interchange of
J
and
K
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}=-{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IK)(IJ)}\,,

(115)

in line with the antisymmetry of the colour factor
T_{IJK}
in eq. (
90
).

We define the scalar integral as our input of the region analysis,

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}=\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}v_{I}\cdot v_{J}v_{I}\cdot v_{K}\int\left[{\cal D}q_{J}\right]\left[{\cal D}q_{K}\right]Y_{(IJ)(IK)}(q_{J},q_{K}),

(116)

and then

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}=\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IK)(IJ)}\right]\,.

(117)

Note that in contrast to the web kinematic function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
which admits eq. (
115
), here the subscript of
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
identifies one of the two diagrams in the web.
The Lee-Pomeransky representation for
\tilde{\cal Y}_{(IJ)(IK)}
is then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\frac{{\cal N}^{2}}{2}v_{I}\cdot v_{J}v_{I}\cdot v_{K}\frac{\Gamma(2-\epsilon)}{\Gamma(-3\epsilon)}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{g_{J}}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{g_{K}}\\
&\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{J}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{K}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{I,J}\int_{0}^{\infty}dx_{I,JK}\left[{\cal P}_{(IJ)(IK)}\right]^{\epsilon-2},\end{split}

(118)

and the polynomial
{\cal P}_{(IJ)(IK)}
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal P}_{(IJ)(IK)}=\,\,&\frac{x_{g_{K}}}{4}x_{J}(x_{I,J}+x_{I,JK})\left(\alpha_{IJ}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)+\frac{x_{g_{J}}}{4}x_{K}x_{I,JK}\left(\alpha_{IK}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IK}}\right)\\
&+\frac{x_{g_{J}}}{4}\left(x_{K}^{2}+x_{I,JK}^{2}\right)+\frac{x_{g_{K}}}{4}\left(x_{J}^{2}+x_{I,J}^{2}+x_{I,JK}^{2}\right)\\
&+\frac{x_{g_{K}}}{2}x_{I,J}x_{I,JK}+x_{g_{J}}x_{g_{K}}\left(x_{J}+x_{K}+x_{I,J}+2x_{I,JK}\right)+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}x_{g_{J}}x_{g_{K}}}\,.\end{split}

(119a)

(a)

The integral
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}

(b)

The integral
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}

:

Region vectors for the integral
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}

Table 4
:

The region vectors
\mathbf{u}^{R}
and the coefficient
n_{R}
identifying the overall scaling of each region,
\lambda^{n_{R}\epsilon}
, of the two-loop diagrams
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
,
\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IK)(IJ)}
and

\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}
. The rightmost column describes the region in momentum space in terms of the modes defined in table
2
. In each case, the loop momentum mode is defined by the lightcone momentum components of the two gluons.

Figure
7
presents the interpretation of the Lee-Pomeransky parameters in relation with the diagrams: figure
7(a)
for the web where only one of the gluons attaches to the (nearly) lightlike line
K
, and figure
7(b)
for the web where both gluons attach to this line.

We now perform the asymptotic expansion in
\lambda
using the geometric method in parameter space with the help of
pySecDec
, and immediately proceed to interpret the
region vectors in momentum space, as we have done for the connected web.

The region vectors corresponding to the
small
\beta_{K}^{2}
expansion of the scalar integral
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
of eq. (
118
), and hence of the web
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
of eq. (
112a
), computed using
pySecDec
, are collected in table
4(a)
. The components of the region vector
\mathbf{u}^{R}
follow the order,

{\bf u}^{R}=\{u_{g_{J}},u_{g_{K}},u_{J},u_{K},u_{I,J},u_{I,JK}\}

(120)

corresponding to the scaling of the propagators
in figure
7(a)
as follows

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\{x_{g_{J}},x_{g_{K}},x_{J},x_{K},x_{I,J},x_{I,JK}\}\xrightarrow{\;{R}\;}\\
&\hskip 50.0pt\{x_{g_{J}}\lambda^{u_{g_{J}}^{R}},x_{g_{K}}\lambda^{u_{g_{K}}^{R}},x_{J}\lambda^{u_{J}^{R}},x_{K}\lambda^{u_{K}^{R}},x_{I,J}\lambda^{u_{I,J}^{R}},x_{I,JK}\lambda^{u_{I,JK}^{R}}\}\,.\end{split}

(121)

Table
4(a)
indicates that there are only four regions in this case, one IR region,
[H,C_{\text{IR}}]
, with
n_{R}=-1
, two neutral regions, having
n_{R}=0
, and a single UV region,
[H_{\text{UV}},C_{N}]
, with
n_{R}=2
.
The reason for this simple region structure is clear: the gluon exchanged between the timelike lines is only indirectly affected by taking the
\beta_{K}^{2}\to 0
limit, and it remains hard, or UV hard, throughout.
A similar expansion is obtained for the

\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}
web in eq. (
112b
), which can be obtained from
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
via the cyclic permutation,
I\to J
,
J\to K
and
K\to I
.

A rather different situation is encountered in the case of the region expansion of the scalar integral
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}
and hence of the web
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}
of eq. (
112c
). Here the region vectors computed using
pySecDec
are collected in table
4(c)
.
The components of the region vector
\mathbf{u}^{R}
follow the order,

{\bf u}^{R}=\{u_{g_{I}},u_{g_{J}},u_{I},u_{J},u_{K,I},u_{K,IJ}\}

(122)

corresponding to the scaling of the propagators
in figure
7(a)
as follows

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\{x_{g_{I}},x_{g_{J}},x_{I},x_{J},x_{K,I},x_{K,IJ}\}\xrightarrow{\;{R}\;}\\
&\hskip 50.0pt\{x_{g_{I}}\lambda^{u_{g_{I}}^{R}},x_{g_{J}}\lambda^{u_{g_{J}}^{R}},x_{I}\lambda^{u_{I}^{R}},x_{J}\lambda^{u_{J}^{R}},x_{K,I}\lambda^{u_{K,I}^{R}},x_{K,IJ}\lambda^{u_{K,IJ}^{R}}\}\,.\end{split}

(123)

Compared to
4(a)
, table
4(c)
displays a much richer region structure, reminiscent of that of the connected web in table
3
. The first four rows of table
4(c)
correspond to IR regions, with either both loops in a IR-collinear mode
[C_{\text{IR}},C_{\text{IR}}]
, with
n_{R}=-2
, or just one of them, with
n_{R}=-1
, the second loop remaining hard or collinear neutral.
The next three rows in table
4(c)
correspond to neutral regions with
n_{R}=0
, while the last two rows correspond to UV ones.

Following the same steps we have taken in
section
4.2.1
in the case of the connected web, we now proceed to
build up the asymptotic expansion for the three web kinematic functions
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}
.
To begin, in analogy with eqs. (
104
) to (
106
), we classify the regions contributing to each web using curly brackets to denote an unordered set of modes.
Based on tables
4(a)
,
4(b)
and
4(c)
, the IR regions kinematic functions for the three webs from are defined as follows. For the
(IJ)(IK)
web:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{[H,C_{\mathrm{IR}}]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IK)(IJ)}^{[C_{\mathrm{IR}},H]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211aCIRH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211bCIRH.pdf}}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(124)

for the
{(JK)(JI)}
web:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{[C_{\mathrm{IR}},H]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(JI)(JK)}^{[H,C_{\mathrm{IR}}]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121bCIRH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121aCIRH.pdf}}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(125)

and, finally, for the
{(KI)(KJ)}
web:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{[C_{\mathrm{IR}},H]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}^{[H,C_{\mathrm{IR}}]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCIRH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bHCIR.pdf}}}\right)\,,\\[4.26773pt]
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{[C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}^{[C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCIRCIR.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCIRCIR.pdf}}}\right)\,,\\[4.26773pt]
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{[C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}^{[C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{N}]}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{[C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{N}]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}^{[C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}]}\right]\\[4.26773pt]
=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCNCIR.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCNCIR.pdf}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCIRCN.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCIRCN.pdf}}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(126)

Similarly, the neutral regions for the three webs, respectively, defined as follows. For the
(IJ)(IK)
web:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{[H,H]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IK)(IJ)}^{[H,H]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211aHH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211bHH.pdf}}}\right)\,,\\[4.26773pt]
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{[H,C_{N}]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IK)(IJ)}^{[C_{N},H]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211aCNH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211bCNH.pdf}}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(127)

For the
{(JK)(JI)}
web:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{[H,H]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(JI)(JK)}^{[H,H]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121bHH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121aHH.pdf}}}\right)\,,\\[4.26773pt]
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(JI)(JK)}^{[H,C_{N}]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121bCNH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121aCNH.pdf}}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(128)

and for the
{(KI)(KJ)}
web:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{[H,H]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}^{[H,H]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aHH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bHH.pdf}}}\right)\,,\\[4.26773pt]
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}-\frac{1}{2}\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCNH.pdf}}}-\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bHCN.pdf}}}\,,\\[4.26773pt]
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{[C_{N},C_{N}]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}^{[C_{N},C_{N}]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCNCN.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCNCN.pdf}}}\right)\,\,.\end{split}

(129)

Lastly, the UV regions, respectively, are defined as follows. For the
(IJ)(IK)
web:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{[H_{\mathrm{UV}},C_{N}]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(IK)(IJ)}^{[C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211aCNHUV.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211bCNHUV.pdf}}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(130)

For the
(JK)(JI)
web:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{[C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(JI)(JK)}^{[H_{\mathrm{UV}},C_{N}]}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121bCNHUV.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121aCNHUV.pdf}}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(131)

and, finally, for the
(KI)(KJ)
web:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{[C_{\mathrm{UV}},H]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}^{[H,C_{\mathrm{UV}}]}\right]+\frac{1}{2}\left[\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{[H,C_{\mathrm{UV}}]}-\tilde{{\mathcal{Y}}}_{(KJ)(KI)}^{[C_{\mathrm{UV}},H]}\right]\\[4.26773pt]
=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCUVH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCUVH.pdf}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aHCUV.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bHCUV.pdf}}}\right)\,.\end{split}

(132)

With the above definitions in place, according to the MoR, the asymptotic expansions of the three webs are, respectively,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}\right]=\,\,&\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}+\lambda^{2\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}\end{split}

(133a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}\right]=\,\,&\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}+\lambda^{2\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}\end{split}

(133b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}\right]=\,\,&\lambda^{-2\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}\\[7.11317pt]
&+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}+\lambda^{2\epsilon}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}\end{split}

(133c)

Let us now turn to discuss the results for the asymptotic expansion of each of the three webs as computed region by region in appendices
C
,
D
and
E
,
and then assembled according to eq. (
133
).

Starting with the
(IJ)(IK)
web,
the hard region
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\{H,H\}}
can be found in eq. (
289
). The leading order is a double pole in
\epsilon
, which will be cancelled by other regions. The IR region,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
, the neutral region,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}
, and the UV region,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}
, can be found in eqs. (
239
), (
264
) and (
297
), respectively. By summing up all the regions contributing to the right-hand side of eq. (
133
), we get the following result:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}\right]=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg\{-\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\left[\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{y_{IJk}}\right)+\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\right]\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\frac{\lambda^{2}}{y_{IJk}}\right)\bigg[M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(134)

where the functions
M_{100}
and
V_{1}
can be found in appendix
A
.
Notice that
\log\left(\lambda\right)
is not completely cancelled in the sum of regions. The result in eq. (
134
) is in agreement with the expansion performed on the original web
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
defined with three timelike lines. The latter was computed in refs.
Mitov:2009sv
;
Ferroglia:2009ii
;
Mitov:2010xw
;
Gardi:2013saa
, and takes the form
13
13

13

In ref.
Gardi:2013saa
, eq. (
134
) is written in terms of a function
S_{1}(\alpha)
as well as ordinary logarithms. The function
S_{1}(\alpha)
defined there is exactly
M_{100}\left(\alpha\right)+V_{1}\left(\alpha\right)
in the present paper.

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left\{\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211IJKa.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211IJKb.pdf}}}\right\}\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\frac{1+\alpha_{IK}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IK}^{2}}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg\{\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\bigg[M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IK}\right)+V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IK}\right)\bigg]\\
&-\log\left(\alpha_{IK}\right)\bigg[M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\,.\end{split}

(135)

The expression for the asymptotic expansion of the second web,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}
, can be obtained from the above by an
(I,J)
permutation. Note that the neutral region
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}
is
(I,J)
symmetric, and therefore it will cancel upon summing up the two webs.

Finally, consider the
(KI)(KJ)
web. We first observe that the hard region
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\{H,H\}}
is even more singular than in the previous cases considered, however, the expression is simpler (see eq. (
291
)),

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\{H,H\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\!\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left[\frac{1}{3}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+\frac{11}{6}\pi^{2}\right]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(136)

The strict limit of this web contains a triple pole in
\epsilon
. Similarly to the connected web,
W_{(IJK)}
, the other neutral regions are vanishing,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\{C_{N},C_{N}\}}={\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\{C_{N},H\}}=0\,.

(137)

In addition to these neutral regions, the purely IR collinear region is also trivial due to the antisymmetry,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=0\,.

(138)

The remaining IR regions of this web,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\}}

are given in eqs. (
258
) and (
242
), respectively, while the UV region
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\}}
can be found in eq. (
304
).
The sum of all the regions of this web is then,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}\right]=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg[\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}-\log^{2}(\lambda)\bigg]\\
&+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(139)

which also matches the small
\beta_{K}^{2}
expansion performed on the expression for the web
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}
with three timelike lines (a permutation of the expression quoted in eq. (
135
) above).

Finally, summing up the asymptotic expansions for the three multiple-gluon-exchange webs we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}\right]=&\\[4.26773pt]
&\hskip-170.71652pt\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\bigg[M_{100}(\alpha_{IJ})+V_{1}(\alpha_{IJ})+2\log(\lambda)\log(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg]\\[4.26773pt]
&\hskip-28.45274pt+\frac{1}{4}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}-\log^{2}(\lambda)\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(140)

where the functions
M_{100}
and
V_{1}
can be found in appendix
A
.

To summarize, we have confirmed for each separate web, that the MoR successfully reproduces the
small-
\beta_{K}^{2}
expansion of the original expression obtained with timelike lines. In particular, all higher-order poles in
\epsilon
cancel between the regions, leaving behind the correct single pole, which eventually contributes to the soft anomalous dimension.

Comparing tables
4(a)
,
4(b)
and
4(c)
to table
3
, we observe that all the loop-momentum modes, and also all their specific pairings into regions for the three web functions
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(KI)(KJ)}
have already been encountered in the case of the connected web
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}
. Because these four webs also share the same colour structure,
\mathbf{T}_{(IJK)}
, this means, in particular, that their contributions from separate regions can be combined into
Region functions

{\cal R}^{R}_{(IJK)}
with the dependence on
\lambda^{n_{R}\epsilon}
factored out, as in eq. (
59
). With these one can further form invariant region functions
\mathbb{R}^{(n_{R})}_{(IJK)}
, as we do in the next section.

4.2.3
Tripole region functions at two loops

Let us now use the results computed in the previous sections to construct the complete asymptotic expansion of the exponent of the correlator in eq. (
19
).
Rather than considering the connected and multiple-gluon-exchange webs separately, we will combine them into ordinary region functions,
{\cal R}^{R}
, and invariant ones,
{\mathbb{R}}^{(n_{R})}
, according to eq. (
59
).

The gauge-invariant region functions for the two-loop colour tripole
\mathbf{T}_{(IJK)}
are given by the asymptotic expansion of all four contributing webs,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}\right]&\\[4.26773pt]
&\hskip-170.71652pt=\sum_{R}\lambda^{n_{R}\epsilon}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{R}=\sum_{n_{R}}\lambda^{n_{R}\epsilon}{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(n_{R})}\\[4.26773pt]
&\hskip-170.71652pt=\lambda^{-2\epsilon}{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(-2)}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(-1)}+{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(0)}+\lambda^{2\epsilon}{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(2)}\,,\end{split}

(141)

with

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(-2)}

\displaystyle={\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}

(142a)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(-1)}

\displaystyle={\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\}}+{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}

(142b)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(0)}

\displaystyle={\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}+{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H\}}+{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},C_{N}\}}

(142c)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(2)}

\displaystyle={\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\}}+{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\}}

(142d)

where the negative, zero and positive superscripts of the invariant region function
\mathbb{R}_{(IJk)}^{(n_{R})}
correspond respectively to IR regions, neutral regions and UV regions.
Note that the subscripts of all region functions are the same: they indicate the colour structure they are associated with,
\mathbf{T}_{(IJK)}
, in contrast to the subscript of
{\mathcal{Y}}
, which correspond to specific webs.

The IR region functions appearing in the first line of eqs. (
142a
) and (
142b
) are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}\end{split}

(143a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}\end{split}

(143b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}\end{split}

(143c)

All of these IR regions are non-trivial and contribute to the correlator. The results of the region functions

{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}
,

{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
and

{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}

can be found in eqs. (
252
), (
243
) and (
259
), respectively.

In eq. (
142c
), the neutral region functions are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}\end{split}

(144a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}\end{split}

(144b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}=\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}\,.\end{split}

(144c)

As discussed in previous sections, the neutral regions except for the hard are vanishing, see eqs. (
107
) and (
137
), so

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(0)}={\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}.

(145)

The result of the hard region
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}
can be found in eq. (
292
).

The two UV region functions in eq. (
142d
) are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}=\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}\end{split}

(146a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\}}=\,\,&{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}\,.\end{split}

(146b)

The results for
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}

and
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}

can be found in eq. (
299
) and (
305
), respectively.
One can check that the two UV regions cancel each other on the correlator level,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(2)}={\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\}}+{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\}}=0\,.

(147)

The UV modes completely disappear as this invariant function
{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(2)}
is vanishing. Note that this is not true on the web-by-web level.

Finally, at the correlator level, accounting for the vanishing UV contribution and all of the trivial neutral regions with the exception of the hard one, the asymptotic expansion reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJK)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}\right]&\\[4.26773pt]
&\hskip-170.71652pt=\lambda^{-2\epsilon}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}\left({\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\}}+{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}\right)+{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}\\
=\lambda^{-2\epsilon}{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(-2)}+\lambda^{-\epsilon}{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(-1)}+{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(0)}\,.\end{split}

(148)

The surviving modes at the correlator level are
H
,
C_{N}
and
C_{\mathrm{IR}}
which have been all observed at one loop. The remaining region functions all contain triple poles in
\epsilon
. The results of the hard region
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}
and the sum of the IR regions at the leading order in power expansion are

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathbb{R}}_{(IJk)}^{(0)}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\log(y_{IJk})\\
