Appendix A
Basis of transcendental functions

In this appendix we define the transcendental functions that are used to express the results for the webs we compute. Since we restrict the computation to
{\cal O}(\epsilon^{-1})
at two loops, we only need polylogarithmic functions up to weight 3. We work with a basis of uniform weight functions, and express them using Goncharov polylogarithms
Goncharov:2009lql
, adopting the notation of
PolyLogTools

Duhr:2019tlz
. These generalize both harmonic
Gehrmann:2000zt
and classical polylogarithms, but the low-weight functions used here can in fact all be expressed as classical polylogarithms.

We classify the functions into three groups, depending on where they appear. First, we require a function from the basis transcendental functions appearing in multiple-gluon-exchange webs,
M_{abc}(\alpha)
, defined in refs.
Gardi:2013saa
;
Falcioni:2014pka
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}M_{100}(\alpha)&=-\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}+4G(-1,0,\alpha)-4G(0,0,\alpha)+4G(1,0,\alpha)\\
&=-\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}+2\text{Li}_{2}\left(\alpha^{2}\right)+4\log\left(\alpha\right)\log\left(1-\alpha^{2}\right)-2\log^{2}\left(\alpha\right)\,.\end{split}

(206)

Next, for the one-loop web computation in eq. (
214
) at
{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})
and
{\cal O}(\epsilon^{1})
we define:

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{1}(\alpha)&\,\equiv-\frac{1}{3}\left[12G\left(-1,0,\alpha\right)-6G\left(0,0,\alpha\right)+\pi^{2}\right]\\
&\,=-4\text{Li}_{2}(-\alpha)+\log^{2}(\alpha)-4\log(\alpha+1)\log(\alpha)-\frac{\pi^{2}}{3}\,,\end{split}

(207a)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}V_{2}(\alpha)\equiv\,\,&-\frac{1}{6}\pi^{2}\left[4G(-1,\alpha)+3G(0,\alpha)\right]-8G(-1,-1,0,\alpha)\\
&+4G(-1,0,0,\alpha)+4G(0,-1,0,\alpha)-2G(0,0,0,\alpha)+4\zeta(3)\,.\end{split}

(207b)

Finally, for the two-loop web computation involving two timelike Wilson lines and one approaching the lightlike limit, we also need functions depending also on the kinematic variable
y_{IJk}
of eq. (
11
), which we defined as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}U_{1}(y_{IJk})\equiv-G\left(0,0,y_{IJk}\right)-\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}\,,\end{split}

(208a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}U_{2}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\equiv\,\,&-4G\left(0,0,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+8G\left(1,0,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+4G\left(0,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)G\left(0,y_{IJk}\right)\\
&+6\pi^{2}G\left(-1,y_{IJk}\right)-\pi^{2}G\left(0,y_{IJk}\right)+12G\left(-1,0,0,y_{IJk}\right)\\
&-2G\left(0,0,0,y_{IJk}\right)-4{\mathbb{U}}\left(\frac{y_{IJk}}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)-4{\mathbb{U}}\left(\alpha_{IJ}y_{IJk}\right)+12\zeta(3)\,,\end{split}

(208b)

where

{\mathbb{U}}(x)=G(-1,0,0,x)+\frac{\pi^{2}}{2}G(-1,x).

(209)

Appendix B
Computation and result for regions at one loop

In this appendix we provide the details on the Feynman integral computation of the region functions for one-loop webs in the limit
\beta_{K}^{2}\to 0
using the MoR.
We perform the asymptotic expansion of the kinematic functions
{\cal Y}
given in section
4.1
using the package
AmpRed

Chen:2024xwt
. Then, each region
{\cal Y}^{R}
can be reduced to a finite set of independent master integrals using integration-by-parts (IBP) identities
Chetyrkin:1981qh
. In practice, we use a combination of the packages
AmpRed

Chen:2024xwt
and
Kira

Maierhofer:2017gsa
;
Klappert:2020nbg
;
Lange:2025fba
to do the reduction. For each master integral
I^{R}
that contributes to the region
R
of a particular web,
{\cal Y}^{R}
, we directly perform the integrations in parametric space. More specifically, we use Feynman parametrization,

\displaystyle I^{R}=\frac{\Gamma\left(\nu-\frac{LD}{2}\right)}{\prod_{e=1}^{E}\Gamma\left(\nu_{e}\right)}\int_{a>0}\left[da\right]\prod_{e=1}^{E}a_{e}^{\nu_{e}-1}\frac{\left({\cal U}\right)^{\nu-\frac{(L+1)D}{2}}}{\left({\cal F}\right)^{\nu-\frac{LD}{2}}}\,,

(210)

where
L
is the number of loops,
E
the number of (internal) edges,
\nu_{e}
is the power of a given propagator and
\nu
is the sum of
\nu_{e}
. The spacetime dimension
D
is set to be
D=4-2\epsilon
through all the calculations. The notation in eq. (
210
) is consistent with that of eq. (
52
) where the integral is expressed using the Lee-Pomeransky representation. The integral (
210
) is defined in projective space, where the measure is

\displaystyle\begin{split}[da]=\prod_{e=1}^{E}da_{e}\delta\left(1-\sum_{e=1}^{E}a_{e}\right)\,.\end{split}

(211)

Owing to the projective space redundancy (the
Cheng-Wu theorem) there is the freedom to set the sum of a subset of parameters to be one, and all the remaining parameters are integrated over all positive values.

The following sections contain the IBP reductions for regions
{\cal Y}^{R}
, the Feynman parameter representations of the master integrals
I^{R}
, as well as the results of
{\cal Y}^{R}
. To facilitate straightforward check of the computation, we will also specify the order of integration we used to evaluate the parametric master integrals which do not simply evaluate into Euler gamma functions.

B.1
Region
{\cal R}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}

The hard region of the
(IJ)
web is clearly not affected by the limit
\beta_{K}^{2}\to 0
. This web is therefore given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}=\,\,&\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IJH.pdf}}}=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}\frac{\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1}{2\alpha_{IJ}}I_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}(\alpha_{IJ})+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(212)

where
{\cal N}
is defined in eq. (
69
) and the integral
I_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}
is defined by

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}=4^{\epsilon+1}\Gamma(1+\epsilon)\int_{a>0}[da]a_{1}^{2\epsilon-1}\left[a_{3}a_{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)+a_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}+4a_{1}\left(a_{2}+a_{3}\right)\right]^{-\epsilon-1}.\end{split}

(213)

By setting
a_{3}=1
(Cheng-Wu theorem) and integrating
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
sequentially, we get the following result

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}={\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}\pi}\right)^{-\epsilon}2\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\Gamma(-\epsilon)\Gamma(2\epsilon)\bigg[(\alpha_{IJ}+1)^{\epsilon}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(1-\epsilon,\epsilon;\epsilon+1;\frac{\alpha_{IJ}}{\alpha_{IJ}+1}\right)\\
&-\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}+1\right)^{\epsilon}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(1-\epsilon,\epsilon;\epsilon+1;\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}+1}\right)\bigg]+{\cal O}(\lambda)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\bigg[-\frac{2}{\epsilon}\log(\alpha_{IJ})+V_{1}(\alpha_{IJ})+\epsilon V_{2}(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{2})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(214)

where we expanded to
{\cal O}(\epsilon^{1})
, since these terms enter the renormalization of the correlator at two loops.

B.2
Region
{\cal R}_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}
and Region
{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{H\}}

In contrast to
{\cal R}_{(IJ)}^{\{H\}}
of section
B.1
, the integrals involving the line
K
,
{\cal R}_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}
and
{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{H\}}
are greatly simplified by taking the limit.
The read

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JkH.pdf}}}=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}\frac{1-2\epsilon}{2\epsilon}I_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(215a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{H\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IKH.pdf}}}=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}\frac{1-2\epsilon}{2\epsilon}I_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(215b)

The integral
I_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}
in parametric space is defined in

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}=4^{\epsilon}\Gamma(\epsilon)\int_{a>0}[da]a_{1}^{2\epsilon-2}\left(4a_{1}a_{2}+a_{2}^{2}\right)^{-\epsilon}.\end{split}

(216)

The integral is easy to perform and the result is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}={\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{H\}}=\,\,&\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}\pi}\right)^{-\epsilon}2\Gamma(-\epsilon)\Gamma(2\epsilon)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\\
=\,\,&\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}-\frac{5\pi^{2}}{12}+\epsilon\frac{7}{3}\zeta(3)\right]+{\cal O}\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\end{split}

(217)

B.3
Region
{\cal R}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{N}\}}
and Region
{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{N}\}}

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{N}\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JKHCN.pdf}}}=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}\frac{1-2\epsilon}{2\epsilon}I_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(218a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{N}\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IKCN.pdf}}}=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}\frac{1-2\epsilon}{2\epsilon}I_{(Jk)}^{\{H\}}+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(218b)

The result is exactly the same as the hard regions,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{N}\}}={\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{N}\}}=\,\,&\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}\pi}\right)^{-\epsilon}2\Gamma(-\epsilon)\Gamma(2\epsilon)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\\
=\,\,&\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\left[-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}-\frac{5\pi^{2}}{12}+\epsilon\frac{7}{3}\zeta(3)\right]+{\cal O}\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\end{split}

(219)

B.4
Region
{\cal R}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}
and Region
{\cal R}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11JKHCIR.pdf}}}=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}(y_{IJk})+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(220a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/11IKCIR.pdf}}}=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}{\cal N}\frac{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}{2}I_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}(y_{IJk}^{-1})+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(220b)

where the integral
I_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}
is defined in

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=4^{\epsilon+1}\Gamma(\epsilon+1)\int_{a>0}[da]a_{1}^{2\epsilon-1}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{2}a_{3}+4a_{1}\left(a_{2}+a_{3}\right)\right]^{-\epsilon-1},\end{split}

(221)

The integral is also easy to perform. The result for this region is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=\,\,&\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}\pi}\right)^{-\epsilon}2\pi\csc(\pi\epsilon)\Gamma(\epsilon)\left(y_{IJk}\right)^{-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\\
=\,\,&\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}-\frac{\log(y_{IJk})}{\epsilon}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+2\pi^{2}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{24}\epsilon\left(-\log^{3}(y_{IJk})-6\pi^{2}\log(y_{IJk})-16\zeta(3)\right)\bigg\}+{\cal O}\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(222a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Ik)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=\,\,&\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\left(\frac{m^{2}}{\mu^{2}\pi}\right)^{-\epsilon}2\pi\csc(\pi\epsilon)\Gamma(\epsilon)\left(y_{IJk}\right)^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\\
=\,\,&\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{\log(y_{IJk})}{\epsilon}+\frac{1}{4}\left(\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+2\pi^{2}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{24}\epsilon\left(\log^{3}(y_{IJk})+6\pi^{2}\log(y_{IJk})-16\zeta(3)\right)\bigg\}+{\cal O}\left(\epsilon^{2}\right)+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(222b)

Appendix C
Computation and result for IR regions at two loops

In this appendix, we provide the computations and the results of the IR regions at two loops. The method we used at two loops is the same at one loop; it is described in the beginning of appendix
B
. For each region
R
, we split the section into three subsections which contain the computations of the connected web, multi-gluon-exchanged webs, as well as a summary presenting the integration order of the non-trivial integrals and the result of the region function
{\cal R}^{R}
.

For the most complicated IR region
\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\}
in appendix
C.1
, we explain the steps in performing the integration. In the remaining sections, we will only provide the initial parametric representations and the order of integration.

C.1
Region
{\cal R}^{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H}_{(IJk)}

In this section, we will provide the main steps of the integration of the non-trivial integrals.

C.1.1
Connected web

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CIRH.pdf}}}\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\left(y_{IJk}-1\right)}{2\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\\
&+\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\left(\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1\right)}{\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}\left[I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})-I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]\\
&+\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{9(\epsilon-1)\epsilon+2}{8\epsilon^{2}}\left[\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I_{(IJk),3}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I_{(IJk),3}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(223)

The master integrals in the Feynman parameter representation are

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2+2\epsilon)\int_{a>0}[da]\left[\left(a_{1}+a_{2}\right)a_{6}\right]^{3\epsilon}\\
&\bigg[4a_{1}a_{6}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)+4a_{2}a_{6}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)+\left(a_{3}^{2}+a_{4}^{2}\right)a_{6}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+a_{3}a_{4}a_{6}\left(\alpha_{IJ}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)+a_{2}a_{3}a_{5}\sqrt{y_{IJk}}+\frac{a_{1}a_{4}a_{5}}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}\bigg]^{-2(\epsilon+1)},\end{split}

(224a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2+2\epsilon)\int_{a>0}[da]a_{5}\left(a_{1}a_{5}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\bigg[a_{5}\left(a_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}\right)+4a_{1}a_{5}\left(a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+a_{5}a_{3}a_{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)+\frac{a_{1}a_{3}a_{4}}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}\bigg]^{-2(\epsilon+1)},\end{split}

(224b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJk),3}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon}\Gamma(2\epsilon)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{4}\right)^{-2+3\epsilon}\\
&\left[a_{2}^{2}a_{4}+4a_{1}a_{4}\left(a_{2}+a_{3}\right)+a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}\sqrt{y_{IJk}}\right]^{-2\epsilon}.\end{split}

(224c)

For
I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
, we set the parameter
a_{4}=1
, and by integrating
a_{5}
,
a_{6}
,
a_{1}
and
a_{2}
sequentially, we get the following integral with
a_{3}
to be integrated,

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&-2^{1-4\epsilon}3\pi\csc(\pi\epsilon)\sqrt{y_{IJk}}\Gamma(-\epsilon)\Gamma(3\epsilon)\\
&\int_{0}^{\infty}da_{3}\frac{\left(a_{3}+1\right)^{-3\epsilon-1}}{a_{3}y_{IJk}-1}\left[a_{3}^{\epsilon}\left(y_{IJk}\right)^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}-\left(y_{IJk}\right)^{-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\right]\left(a_{3}+\alpha_{IJ}\right)^{\epsilon}\left(a_{3}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)^{\epsilon}.\end{split}

(225)

This integral is not easy to get a close form in
\epsilon
. Fortunately, the integrand has a regular behaviour for both large and small
a_{3}
. Therefore, it is safe to expand the integrand in
\epsilon
before the integration. We then perform the expansion, together with the variable transformation
a_{3}\rightarrow\frac{x}{1-x}
, getting

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\left(y_{IJk}-1\right)}{2\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\int_{0}^{1}dx\frac{y_{IJk}-1}{xy_{IJk}+x-1}\log\left(\frac{xy_{IJk}}{1-x}\right)\\
&\bigg\{\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[2\log\left(x\alpha_{IJ}-x+1\right)+2\log\left(1-x+\frac{x}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt+\log(1-x)+\log(x)\bigg]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\bigg\}.\end{split}

(226)

Now the integral at each order of
\epsilon
is evaluated to generalized polylogarithms. Notice that the corresponding prefactor in eq. (
223
) has been included in eq. (
226
). Using the package
PolyLogTools

Duhr:2019tlz
, we obtained the result of the first two orders,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\left(y_{IJk}-1\right)}{2\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}\bigg[\frac{2}{\epsilon^{2}}U_{1}\left(y_{IJk}\right)+\frac{1}{2\epsilon}U_{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}\right)+O\left(\epsilon^{0}\right)\bigg].\end{split}

(227)

The functions
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
can be found in appendix
A
.

For the integral
I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
, we set
a_{2}=1
and integrate
a_{5}
,
a_{1}
and
a_{4}
sequentially, leaving behind
a_{3}
as the final variable to be integrated,

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&4^{1-2\epsilon}\epsilon\Gamma(-\epsilon)^{2}\Gamma(3\epsilon)\Gamma(\epsilon+1)\left(y_{IJk}\right)^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\\
&\int_{0}^{\infty}da_{3}a_{3}^{\epsilon}\left(a_{3}+1\right)^{-3\epsilon}\left(a_{3}+\alpha_{IJ}\right)^{\epsilon-1}\left(a_{3}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)^{\epsilon-1}.\end{split}

(228)

We then consider the
(I,J)
antisymmetric combination, which is how

I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
appears in eq. (
223
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\left(\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1\right)}{\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}\left[I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})-I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]=\,\,&\\
&\hskip-170.71652pt{\cal N}^{2}4^{1-2\epsilon}\frac{\left(\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1\right)}{\alpha_{IJ}}\Gamma(-\epsilon)^{2}\Gamma(3\epsilon)\Gamma(\epsilon+1)\left(\left(y_{IJk}\right)^{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}-\left(y_{IJk}\right)^{-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\right)\\
&\hskip-142.26378pt\int_{0}^{\infty}da_{3}a_{3}^{\epsilon}\left(a_{3}+1\right)^{-3\epsilon}\left(a_{3}+\alpha_{IJ}\right)^{\epsilon-1}\left(a_{3}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)^{\epsilon-1}.\end{split}

(229)

Notice that, similarly to eq. (
225
), the integrand is regular for both small and large values of
a_{3}
. Next, we change the variables according to
a_{3}\rightarrow\frac{x}{1-x}
and expand the integrand in
\epsilon
. Finally, we get the result in terms of generalized polylogarithms,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\left(\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1\right)}{\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}\left[I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})-I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]=\,\,&\\
&\hskip-284.52756pt\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)\bigg\{\frac{8}{3\epsilon^{2}}\log(\alpha_{IJ})-\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{2}{3}M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+2V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\bigg]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\bigg\}.\end{split}

(230)

The function
V_{1}
can be found in appendix
A
.

Compared to the two complicated integrals discussed above, the integral
I_{(IJk),3}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
is straightforward to evaluate and we will present the result directly. Using the command
AlphaIntEvaluate
provided in the package
AmpRed

Chen:2024xwt
, we get the following result containing simply ordinary logarithms,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{9(\epsilon-1)\epsilon+2}{8\epsilon^{2}}\left[\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I_{(IJk),3}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I_{(IJk),3}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]=\,\,&\\
&\hskip-284.52756pt\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{-\frac{2}{3\epsilon^{3}}\log(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{36\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\left[\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+24\pi^{2}\right]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\bigg\}\end{split}

(231)

Finally, by summing up the results in eqs. (
227
), (
230
) and (
231
), we get the kinematic function contributing to the region
\{H,C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}
of the connected web,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{-\frac{2}{3\epsilon^{3}}\log(y_{IJk})\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg[\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\frac{8}{3}\log(y_{IJk})\log(\alpha_{IJ})+\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}2U_{1}(y_{IJk})\bigg]\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[-\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)\bigg(\frac{4}{3}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+2V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\bigg)\\
&-\log(y_{IJk})\bigg(\frac{1}{36}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+\frac{2\pi^{2}}{3}\bigg)+\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}\frac{1}{2}U_{2}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(232)

C.1.2
Multiple-gluon-exchange webs

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121bCIRH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121aCIRH.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\left(\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1\right)}{8\alpha_{IJ}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}\left[I_{(Jk)(JI),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})-I_{(IJ)(Jk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\right]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(233a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211aCIRH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211bCIRH.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&-\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\left(\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1\right)}{8\alpha_{IJ}}\sqrt{y_{IJk}}\left[I_{(Jk)(JI),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}^{-1})-I_{(IJ)(Jk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(233b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCIRH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bHCIR.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{2\epsilon-1}{8\epsilon}\left[\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(233c)

We will only compute
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
, since
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
, can be obtained from it by performing an
(I,J)
interchange. The web in eq. (
233c
), where both gluons connect to the lightlike line, is of course different to the other two, and will be computed as well.

The Feynman parameter representation of the master integrals is given below.

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(Jk)(JI),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\bigg[a_{2}a_{3}^{2}+a_{2}\left(a_{4}+a_{6}\right)^{2}+\left(a_{4}+a_{6}\right)a_{2}a_{3}\left(\alpha_{IJ}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}+2a_{6}\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{1}a_{5}a_{6}\bigg]^{-2(\epsilon+1)},\end{split}

(234a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(Jk)(JI),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\bigg[a_{2}\left(a_{3}^{2}+a_{6}^{2}\right)+a_{2}a_{6}a_{3}\left(\alpha_{IJ}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}+2a_{6}\right)+a_{1}a_{5}\left(a_{4}+a_{6}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}\bigg]^{-2(\epsilon+1)},\end{split}

(234b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon+1}\Gamma(2\epsilon+1)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon-1}\\
&\left[a_{1}a_{4}^{2}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)+a_{2}a_{3}a_{5}\sqrt{y_{IJk}}\right]^{-2\epsilon-1}.\end{split}

(234c)

For
I_{(Jk)(JI),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
, we set
a_{4}+a_{6}=1
and integrate
a_{5}
,
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
and
a_{6}
sequentially. Note that the integral range over
a_{6}
is from
0
to
1
. The final integration over
a_{3}
, remains complicated due to the hypergeometric function appearing in the integrand,

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(Jk)(JI),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=&-4^{2-2\epsilon}\frac{\pi^{2}\csc^{2}(\pi\epsilon)\Gamma(3\epsilon)}{\Gamma(\epsilon+1)}\left(y_{IJk}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\\
&\int_{0}^{\infty}da_{3}\left(a_{3}+1\right)^{-3\epsilon}\left(a_{3}+\alpha_{IJ}\right)^{\epsilon-1}\left(a_{3}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)^{\epsilon-1}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\epsilon,3\epsilon;\epsilon+1;-\frac{1}{a_{3}+1}\right).\end{split}

(235)

Let us examine the behaviour of the integrand near the integration boundary. We find that the hypergeometric function is finite at small and large values of
a_{3}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\alpha_{3}\rightarrow 0}\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\epsilon,3\epsilon;\epsilon+1;-\frac{1}{a_{3}+1}\right)=\,\,&\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\epsilon,3\epsilon;\epsilon+1;-1\right)+{\cal O}(a_{3}),\end{split}

(236a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\alpha_{3}\rightarrow\infty}\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(\epsilon,3\epsilon;\epsilon+1;-\frac{1}{a_{3}+1}\right)=\,\,&1+O\left(\frac{1}{a_{3}}\right).\end{split}

(236b)

Furthermore, the product of rational factors in the integrand of eq. (
235
) is also regular in these two limits of
a_{3}
. Thus, the entire integrand is finite in both limits, and therefore
\epsilon
is not necessarily there as a regulator. We perform the variable transformation
a_{3}\rightarrow\frac{x}{1-x}
and expand the integrand in
\epsilon
, similarly to how we proceeeded in appendix
C.1.1
. The integral is then evaluated in terms of generalized polylogarithms. We will present the result after combining the two integrals
I_{(Jk)(JI),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
and
I_{(Jk)(JI),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
, since it is simpler than each one of two individually.

For the integral
I_{(Jk)(JI),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
, we set
a_{6}=1
. We integrate
a_{5}
,
a_{1}
,
a_{2}
and
a_{4}
sequentially, and the remaining integral of
a_{3}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(Jk)(JI),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=&-4^{2-2\epsilon}\pi^{2}\csc^{2}(\pi\epsilon)\left(y_{IJk}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\\
&\int_{0}^{\infty}da_{3}\left(a_{3}+\alpha_{IJ}\right)^{\epsilon-1}\left(a_{3}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)^{\epsilon-1}\\
&\left[\left(a_{3}+1\right)^{-2\epsilon}\Gamma(2\epsilon)-\frac{\left(a_{3}+2\right)^{-3\epsilon}\Gamma(3\epsilon)}{\Gamma(\epsilon+1)}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(1,3\epsilon;\epsilon+1;\frac{1}{a_{3}+2}\right)\right].\end{split}

(237)

The asymptotic behaviour of the hypergeometric function is regular in both limits,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\alpha_{3}\rightarrow 0}\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,3\epsilon;\epsilon+1;\frac{1}{a_{3}+2}\right)=\,\,&\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,3\epsilon;\epsilon+1;\frac{1}{2}\right)+{\cal O}(a_{3}),\end{split}

(238a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\alpha_{3}\rightarrow\infty}\,{}_{2}F_{1}\left(1,3\epsilon;\epsilon+1;\frac{1}{a_{3}+2}\right)=\,\,&1+O\left(\frac{1}{a_{3}}\right),\end{split}

(238b)

so we can again expand the integrand in
\epsilon
and perform the integration order by order. Finally, the result of the kinematic function contributing to the region
\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\}
is given below,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JI)(Jk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\bigg\{-\frac{4}{3\epsilon^{3}}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{1}{3}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)}+\frac{2}{3}M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+V_{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\right]\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[-\frac{8}{3}G\left(-1,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{4}{3}G\left(-1,0,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{16}{3}G\left(-1,1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&-\frac{4}{3}G\left(0,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{16}{3}G\left(0,1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+4G\left(0,\frac{1}{2},0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{16}{3}G\left(1,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{4}{3}G\left(1,0,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{16}{3}G\left(1,1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&-4G\left(1,2,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-4G\left(1,\frac{1}{2},0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+4\log(2)G\left(0,\frac{1}{2},\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+4\log(2)G\left(1,2,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-4\log(2)G\left(1,\frac{1}{2},\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{1}{9}\log^{3}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&-\log(2)\log^{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+2\log^{2}(2)\log\left(1-\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{8}{9}\pi^{2}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{8}{9}\pi^{2}\log\left(1-\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{10}{9}\pi^{2}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}+1\right)\\
&-{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{1}{6}\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)\left(2M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+3V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\right)}-\frac{1}{12}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\log^{2}\left(y_{IJk}\right)\\
&+\log(2)\left(-M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)-V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\right)+\frac{37\zeta(3)}{6}-\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\log(2)\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(239)

The same region of the other web
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
can be obtained by performing
(I,J)
permutation,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}\right)=-{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JI)(Jk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}^{-1}\right)\,.

(240)

Most of the terms appearing in eq. (
239
) are
(I,J)
symmetric, while only the two terms coloured in red are antisymmetric and will survive in the sum of the two webs,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(JK)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{-\frac{2}{3\epsilon^{2}}\log(\alpha_{IJ})\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{2}{3}M_{100}(\alpha_{IJ})+V_{1}(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(241)

The integral
I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
is straightforward to evaluate, and the result of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{\!-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}-\frac{1}{24\epsilon}\left[\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+16\pi^{2}\right]+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(242)

C.1.3
Summary for
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}

The procedure we used to compute the non-trivial master integrals is summarized in table

.

.

Upon summing the webs according to (

) the
\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\}
region
contribution to the correlators is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{-\frac{5}{3\epsilon^{3}}\log(y_{IJk})\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg[2\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\log(\alpha_{IJ})+\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}2U_{1}(y_{IJk})\bigg]\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[-\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})V_{1}(\alpha_{IJ})+\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}\frac{1}{2}U_{2}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt-\frac{5}{72}\log^{3}(y_{IJk})-\frac{4}{3}\pi^{2}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(243)

C.2
Region
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}

C.2.1
Connected web

Decomposing the double collinear-IR region of the connected web into master integrals we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CIRCIR.pdf}}}\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}{1+y_{IJk}}\left[I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})-I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]\\
&+\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{2\epsilon-1}{8\epsilon}\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(244)

The Feynman parameter representation of the master integrals is

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})=&-\Gamma(2\epsilon+1)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{2}a_{5}+a_{1}a_{2}+a_{1}a_{5}\right)^{3\epsilon-1}\\
&\bigg[a_{2}a_{5}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}\right)+a_{1}\left(a_{2}+a_{5}\right)\left(a_{3}+a_{4}\right)+\frac{1}{4\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{1}a_{3}a_{4}\bigg]^{-2\epsilon-1},\end{split}

(245a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})=&-4^{2\epsilon+1}\Gamma(2\epsilon+1)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon-1}\\
&\left[a_{2}a_{3}a_{5}\sqrt{y_{IJk}}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{1}a_{4}a_{5}\right]^{-2\epsilon-1}.\end{split}

(245b)

The result of the function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\log(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}U_{1}(y_{IJk})\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg[\frac{1}{6}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+\frac{7}{6}\pi^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(246)

C.2.2
Multiple-gluon-exchange webs

Decomposing the double collinear-IR region of the multiple-gluon-exchange web
W_{(kI)(kJ)}
into master integrals we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCIRCIR.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCIRCIR.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{1}{8}\left[I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})-I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(247)

The Feynman parameter representation of the master integral is

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\hskip-56.9055pt\bigg[a_{2}a_{3}a_{6}\sqrt{y_{IJk}}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}+2a_{6}\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{1}a_{4}\left(a_{5}+a_{6}\right)\bigg]^{-2(\epsilon+1)}.\end{split}

(248)

By performing the variable transformation

\displaystyle a_{1}\rightarrow b_{3}\equiv\sqrt{y_{IJk}}a_{1}\,,\qquad a_{2}\rightarrow b_{2}\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{2}\,,

(249)

one finds that the integrand is independent on
y_{IJk}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{0}^{\infty}db_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}db_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}da_{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}da_{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}da_{5}\delta(1-a_{5})\\
&\left(b_{1}b_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\bigg[b_{2}a_{3}a_{6}+4b_{1}b_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}+2a_{6}\right)+b_{1}a_{4}\left(a_{5}+a_{6}\right)\bigg]^{-2(\epsilon+1)}\\
=\,\,&\text{constant}\,.\end{split}

(250)

Notice that we have used the Cheng-Wu theorem to replace the delta function
\delta(1-\sum_{i=1}^{5}a_{i})
by
\delta(1-a_{5})
. Owing to the antisymmetry in eq. (
247
), we conclude that this web is trivial in this region,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=0.\end{split}

(251)

equals the result of the connected web given in eq. (

),

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\log(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}U_{1}(y_{IJk})\\

C.3
Region
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}

C.3.1
Connected web

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CNCIRa.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CNCIRb.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{9(\epsilon-1)\epsilon+2}{8\epsilon^{2}}\left[\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda)\end{split}

(253)

The Feynman parameter representation of the master integral is

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon}\Gamma(2\epsilon)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{4}\right)^{-2+3\epsilon}\\
&\left[a_{2}^{2}a_{4}+4a_{1}a_{4}\left(a_{2}+a_{3}\right)+a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}\sqrt{y_{IJk}}\right]^{-2\epsilon},\end{split}

(254)

Note that this integral is the same as
I_{(IJk),3}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{IR}},H\right\}}(y_{IJk})
; see eq. (
224c
), so we directly write down the result:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{-\frac{2}{3\epsilon^{3}}-\frac{1}{36\epsilon}\left[\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+24\pi^{2}\right]\bigg\}\\
&+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(255)

C.3.2
Multiple-gluon-exchange webs

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCNCIR.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCNCIR.pdf}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCIRCN.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCIRCN.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{1}{8\epsilon}\bigg[\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}_{(kI)(kJ),1}(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}_{(kI)(kJ),1}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\bigg]\\
&+\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{2\epsilon-1}{8\epsilon^{2}}\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}_{(kI)(kJ),2}(y_{IJk})-\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}_{(kI)(kJ),2}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\bigg]\\
&+\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{9(\epsilon-1)\epsilon+2}{16\epsilon^{2}}\bigg[\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}_{(kI)(kJ),3}(y_{IJk})-\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}_{(kI)(kJ),3}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\bigg]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(256)

The Feynman parameter representations of these master integrals are

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})=&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}[da]a_{5}\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\hskip-28.45274pt\left[a_{1}a_{5}^{2}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+2a_{5}\right)+a_{2}a_{3}\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)\sqrt{y_{IJk}}\right]^{-2(\epsilon+1)},\end{split}

(257a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(kI)(kJ),2}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})=&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}[da]a_{5}\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\hskip-28.45274pt\left[a_{2}\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)^{2}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+2a_{5}\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{1}a_{3}a_{5}\right]^{-2(\epsilon+1)},\end{split}

(257b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(kI)(kJ),3}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}(y_{IJk})=&-4^{2\epsilon}\Gamma(2\epsilon)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{-2+3\epsilon}\\
&\left[a_{2}a_{4}^{2}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+2a_{4}\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{1}a_{3}a_{4}\right]^{-2\epsilon}.\end{split}

(257c)

The result of the function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{\mathrm{IR}}\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{\frac{1}{3\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left[\frac{1}{72}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+\frac{1}{3}\pi^{2}\right]\bigg\}\\
&+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(258)

Appendix D
Computation and result for neutral regions at two loops

In this appendix, we present the computations and the results for neutral regions at two loops. We follow the method described in appendix
B
, and organise the results similarly to appendix
C
.

For the hard region of the connected webs summarized in appendix
D.3.1
, we use the method of differential equations
Henn:2013pwa
to evaluate the master integrals. We use the packages
Kira

Maierhofer:2017gsa
;
Klappert:2020nbg
;
Lange:2025fba
and
LiteRed

Lee:2012cn
;
Lee:2013mka
to perform the IBP reduction and derive the differential equations respectively.

D.1
Region
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}

Each web in this region is trivial, because the neutral collinear modes will only extract the anti-lightcone direction of the external velocities; see section
4.3
.

D.1.1
Connected web

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CNCN.pdf}}}=0\,.\end{split}

(260a)

D.1.2
Multiple-gluon-exchange webs

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCNCN.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCNCN.pdf}}}\right)=0\,.\end{split}

(260b)

D.1.3
Summary for
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}

The region function
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}
is vanishing,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},C_{N}\right\}}=0\,.\end{split}

(261)

D.2
Region
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}

D.2.1
Connected web

The connected web if this region is trivial due to the
(I,J)
antisymmetry,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111HCNap.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111HCNbp.pdf}}}\right)=0\,.\end{split}

(262a)

D.2.2
Multiple-gluon-exchange webs

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211aCNH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211bCNH.pdf}}}\right)=-\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1}{4\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}I_{(IJ)(Ik),1}^{\{C_{N},H\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(262b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121bCNH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121aCNH.pdf}}}\right)=\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1}{4\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}I_{(IJ)(Ik),1}^{\{C_{N},H\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(262c)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=&\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCNH.pdf}}}-\frac{1}{2}\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bHCN.pdf}}}=0\,.\end{split}

(262d)

The Feynman parameter representation of the master integral is

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJ)(Ik),1}^{\{C_{N},H\}}=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}\left[da\right]a_{2}\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\left[\frac{a_{2}\left(a_{3}\alpha_{IJ}+a_{4}\right)\left(a_{4}\alpha_{IJ}+a_{3}\right)}{\alpha_{IJ}}+a_{1}a_{5}^{2}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)\right]^{-2(\epsilon+1)}\,.\end{split}

(263)

The result of the function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{1}{2\epsilon^{2}}V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[4G\left(-1,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-2G\left(-1,0,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-2G\left(0,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{6}\log^{3}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{1}{3}\pi^{2}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}+1\right)-2\zeta(3)\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(264)

Notice that the result is symmetric under the
(I,J)
interchange. Therefore, the sum of the two webs is trivial,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=0\,.

(265)

D.2.3
Summary for
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}

The integration order of the master integral appearing in this region is summarized in table
8
.

Integral

Cheng-Wu

Integration orders

I_{(IJ)(Ik),1}^{\{C_{N},H\}}

a_{5}=1

Table 8
:
The master integral of the
\{C_{N},H\}
region associated with the tripole colour structure, specifying the integration measure in the middle column and the order of integration in the rightmost column.

The region function
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}
is also trivial due to the
(I,J)
antisymmetry,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H\right\}}=0.\end{split}

(266)

D.3
Region
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}

D.3.1
Connected web

For the hard region of the connected web, we will use the method of differential equations
Henn:2013pwa
to compute the integrals.

As the strict lightcone limit of the timelike web, we simply replace the time timelike velocity
\beta_{K}
by the lightlike
\beta_{k}
in the integrand in eq. (
91
) at the leading order in
\lambda
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111HH.pdf}}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\int\frac{d^{D}k_{I}}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\int\frac{d^{D}k_{J}}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\int\frac{d^{D}k_{K}}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\frac{1}{k_{I}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{J}^{2}}\frac{1}{(k_{I}+k_{J})^{2}}\\
&\frac{v_{I}^{\mu}}{v_{I}\cdot k_{I}-1}\frac{v_{J}^{\nu}}{v_{J}\cdot k_{J}-1}\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{k}^{\rho}}{-\tilde{\beta}_{k}\cdot(k_{I}+k_{J})}\\
&\left[g_{\mu\nu}\left(k_{I}-k_{J}\right)_{\rho}+g_{\nu\rho}\left(2k_{J}+k_{I}\right)_{\mu}-g_{\rho\mu}\left(2k_{I}+k_{J}\right)_{\nu}\right]+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(267)

For the lightlike velocity
\beta_{k}
, the normalized velocity is not well defined due to the vanishing virtuality. For convenience we then define
\tilde{\beta}_{k}
as follows,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tilde{\beta}_{k}^{\nu}=\frac{\beta_{k}^{\nu}}{-\beta_{k}\cdot\beta_{J}}.\end{split}

(268)

With this definition, the scalar products are

\displaystyle\begin{split}v_{I}^{2}=v_{J}^{2}=1,\qquad v_{I}\cdot v_{J}=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}+\alpha_{IJ}\right),\\
v_{I}\cdot\tilde{\beta}_{k}=-y_{IJk},\qquad v_{J}\cdot\tilde{\beta}_{k}=-1,\qquad\tilde{\beta}_{k}^{2}=0.\end{split}

(269)

Having defined the rescaled velocities as above,
\{\tilde{\beta}_{k},v_{I},v_{J}\}
, rescaling invariance is manifest.

As the starting point for deriving the set of differential equations, we define the integral family which includes the propagators in eq. (
267
) as well as three auxiliary scalar products,

\displaystyle\begin{split}J_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3},\nu_{4},\nu_{5},\nu_{6},\nu_{7},\nu_{8},\nu_{9}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\int\frac{d^{D}k_{I}}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\int\frac{d^{D}k_{J}}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\\
&\hskip-85.35826pt\frac{\left[v_{J}\cdot k_{I}\right]^{-\nu_{7}}\left[\tilde{\beta}_{k}\cdot k_{J}\right]^{-\nu_{8}}\left[v_{I}\cdot k_{J}\right]^{-\nu_{9}}}{\left[k_{I}^{2}\right]^{\nu_{1}}\left[k_{J}^{2}\right]^{\nu_{2}}\left[(k_{I}+k_{J})^{2}\right]^{\nu_{3}}\left[v_{I}\cdot k_{I}-1\right]^{\nu_{4}}\left[v_{J}\cdot k_{J}-1\right]^{\nu_{5}}\left[-\tilde{\beta}_{k}\cdot(k_{I}+k_{J})\right]^{\nu_{6}}}.\end{split}

(270)

After the IBP reduction, the integrals in this family are expressed as linear combinations of eleven master integrals,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}J_{1}\\
J_{2}\\
J_{3}\\
J_{4}\\
J_{5}\\
J_{6}\\
J_{7}\\
J_{8}\\
J_{9}\\
J_{10}\\
J_{11}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}J_{0,1,1,1,0,0,0,0,0}\\
J_{1,1,0,1,1,0,0,0,0}\\
J_{0,1,1,1,1,0,0,0,0}\\
J_{0,1,1,2,1,0,0,0,0}\\
J_{1,1,1,1,1,0,0,0,0}\\
J_{1,1,0,1,1,1,0,0,0}\\
J_{1,1,0,1,1,2,0,0,0}\\
J_{1,1,1,1,1,1,0,0,0}\\
J_{1,1,1,1,1,1,-1,0,0}\\
J_{1,1,1,1,1,1,0,-1,0}\\
J_{1,1,1,1,1,1,0,0,-1}\,,\end{array}\right)\end{split}

(271)

with the top sector defined by the last four master integrals in this eleven dimensional basis.

The subsectors have been transformed into canonical form for the fully timelike case in ref.
Milloy:2020hzi
. For the one-regulator case, the top sector can also be transformed into canonical form
Henn:2023pqn
. Making use of this previous work, along with the package
CANONICA

Meyer:2017joq
, we find the following Uniformly Transcendental (UT) basis for this system,

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{(3-2\epsilon(8\epsilon(2\epsilon-3)+11))}{32\epsilon^{3}}J_{1},\end{split}

(272a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{(3-2\epsilon(8\epsilon(2\epsilon-3)+11))}{3\epsilon^{3}}J_{1}+\frac{(1-2\epsilon)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}J_{2},\end{split}

(272b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),3}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&-\frac{2(2\epsilon(4\epsilon-5)+3)\left(\alpha_{IJ}+1\right)}{3\epsilon^{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}-1\right)}J_{1}-\frac{2(1-2\epsilon)^{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}+1\right)}{3\epsilon^{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}-1\right)}J_{3}\\
&+\frac{(3\epsilon-1)\left(\alpha_{IJ}^{2}-1\right)}{3\epsilon^{2}\alpha_{IJ}}J_{4},\end{split}

(272c)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),4}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{(2\epsilon(4\epsilon-5)+3)\left((4\epsilon-1)\alpha_{IJ}-6\epsilon+1\right)}{6\epsilon^{3}\left(\alpha_{IJ}-1\right)}J_{1}\\
&-\frac{(1-2\epsilon)^{2}}{3\epsilon^{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}-1\right)}J_{3}-\frac{\left(\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}+3\epsilon-1\right)}{6\epsilon^{2}\alpha_{IJ}}J_{4},\end{split}

(272d)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),5}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&-\frac{\left(1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}\right)}{2\alpha_{IJ}}J_{5},\end{split}

(272e)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),6}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{(1-2\epsilon)^{2}y_{IJk}}{8\epsilon^{2}\left(y_{IJk}-1\right)}J_{2}+\frac{(1-4\epsilon)y_{IJk}}{4\epsilon\left(y_{IJk}-1\right)}J_{6}+\frac{y_{IJk}^{2}}{\epsilon\left(y_{IJk}-1\right)}J_{7},\end{split}

(272f)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),7}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{3y_{IJk}}{16\epsilon}J_{7},\end{split}

(272g)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),8}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(y_{IJk}+1\right)J_{8},\end{split}

(272h)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),9}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&y_{IJk}J_{8}+y_{IJk}J_{9},\end{split}

(272i)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),10}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&-\frac{\left(1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}\right)}{2\alpha_{IJ}}J_{10},\end{split}

(272j)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\\
I_{(IJk),11}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&J_{11}+J_{8}.\end{split}

(272k)

With the above UT basis, the differential equation takes the canonical form,

\displaystyle d\overrightarrow{I}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\epsilon\sum_{i}d\log(\omega_{i})\mathbf{C}_{i}\overrightarrow{I}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}},

(273)

where
\overrightarrow{I}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}
is a vector whose components are the eleven UT basis elements above, and
\mathbf{C}_{i}
is a constant matrix associated with the symbol letter
\omega_{i}
. The alphabet of this system is,

\displaystyle\{\omega_{i}\}=\left\{\alpha_{IJ}+1,\alpha_{IJ},1-\alpha_{IJ},\alpha_{IJ}+y_{IJk},\alpha_{IJ}y_{IJk}+1,y_{IJk}+1,y_{IJk}\right\}.

(274)

The constant matrices
\mathbf{C}_{i}
are stored in the
Mathematica
notebook
CanonicalForm.nb
given in ref.
MathematicaNoteBook
. In the notebook, the matrices are given by
dlog2RegHH
following the order of the alphabet
alpha2RegHH
.

The combination of integrals in which we are interested, namely the connected web of eq. (
267
), may be written in terms of UT basis as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}I_{(IJk),8}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\\
&-\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\left[I_{(IJk),5}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ})-2I_{(IJk),10}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\right]\\
&+2\left[I_{(IJk),9}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})-I_{(IJk),11}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\right].\end{split}

(275)

The solution of the differential equation presented in eq. (
273
) can be formally written as the following path-ordered exponential,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\overrightarrow{I}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})=\text{P}\exp\left[\epsilon\sum_{i}\mathbf{C}_{i}\int_{\gamma_{\text{C}}}d\log(\omega_{i})\right]\overrightarrow{I}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}(\bar{\alpha}_{IJ},\bar{y}_{IJk}),\end{split}

(276)

where
\gamma_{\text{C}}
is the integral contour defined in the two-dimensional space spanned by the two kinematic variables, starting from a certain point
(\bar{\alpha}_{IJ},\bar{y}_{IJk})
where the boundary values are given, to the general point
(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})
. In this calculation, the contour is chosen to be

\displaystyle\begin{split}\gamma_{\text{C}}:(0,0)\rightarrow(0,y_{IJk})\rightarrow(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}).\end{split}

(277)

The next step is to determine the boundary values. We use the following three conditions to fix all the boundary values.

Single-pole condition

The sum of all the regions must reproduce the lightcone expansion performed on the timelike result
{\cal T}_{\lambda}\left[{\cal Y}_{(IJK)}\right]
, which can only have a single pole in
\epsilon
, corresponding to the overall UV singularity generated by shrinking the entire web towards the interaction vertex of the Wilson lines.
This requires that all the higher order poles appearing in the individual regions, cancel out upon summing up the region integrals,

\displaystyle\sum_{R}{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{R}={\cal O}(\epsilon^{-1})\,.

(278)

Defining the expansion coefficients of
{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}
in
\epsilon
,

\displaystyle{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}=\sum_{l}{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\},(l)}\epsilon^{l}\,,

(279)

the above condition fixes the leading order of
{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}
, which is a double pole in
\epsilon
, as well as all full-depth terms in the single pole
{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}
, leaving behind some undetermined coefficients of
\pi^{2}
and
\zeta(3)
.

Straight-line limit

By taking the physical kinematic limit where the two timelike Wilson lines are along the same direction, but one is in the initial state and the other in the final state,

\beta_{I}\rightarrow-\beta_{J}
, the kinematic variables become

\displaystyle\alpha_{IJ}\rightarrow 1\,,\qquad y_{IJk}\rightarrow-1\,.

(280)

We then perform the asymptotic expansion around this point by setting
\alpha_{IJ}\equiv 1+\alpha\delta
and
y_{IJk}\equiv-1+y\delta
with
\delta\to 0
. To this end we use the package
AmpRed
, starting with the parametric representation of the integral
{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}
, finding that this straight-line limit yields a pure power expansion in
\delta
, with no additional logarithms. We can therefore compute the integral given in eq. (
267
) in the strict straight-line limit, i.e.,
\alpha_{IJ}=1
and
y_{IJk}=-1
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}\bigg|_{\alpha_{IJ}=1,\,y_{IJk}=-1}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\int\frac{d^{D}k_{I}}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\int\frac{d^{D}k_{J}}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\int\frac{d^{D}k_{K}}{i\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\frac{1}{k_{I}^{2}}\frac{1}{k_{J}^{2}}\frac{1}{(k_{I}+k_{J})^{2}}\\
&\frac{-v_{J}^{\mu}}{-v_{J}\cdot k_{I}-1}\frac{v_{J}^{\nu}}{v_{J}\cdot k_{J}-1}\frac{\tilde{\beta}_{k}^{\rho}}{-\tilde{\beta}_{k}\cdot(k_{I}+k_{J})}\\
&\left[g_{\mu\nu}\left(k_{I}-k_{J}\right)_{\rho}+g_{\nu\rho}\left(2k_{J}+k_{I}\right)_{\mu}-g_{\rho\mu}\left(2k_{I}+k_{J}\right)_{\nu}\right]+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(281)

Moreover, we find that the integral vanishes in this limit after the IBP reduction is performed, i.e. we have

\displaystyle{\cal T}_{\delta}\left[{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}\right]=0\,.

(282)

This, together with eq. (
278
) allows us to fix all the boundary values, except for a single term proportional to
\pi^{2}
.

First-entry condition

On the principal sheet, the unphysical symbol letters,
\alpha_{IJ}+y_{IJk}
and
1+\alpha_{IJ}y_{IJk}
, should not appear as branch points. Given that the result is written in terms of generalized polylogarithms, this property can be checked by taking the discontinuity around the unphysical symbol letter
\omega_{i}
using the coaction, see e.g.
Gaiotto:2011dt
;
Duhr:2012fh
;
Abreu:2014cla
;
Duhr:2019tlz
. We decompose the function
{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}
as follows,

\displaystyle{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}={\cal Y}_{(IJk),0}^{\{H,H\},(-1),}+\pi^{2}{\cal Y}_{(IJk),2}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}+\zeta(3){\cal Y}_{(IJk),3}^{\{H,H\},(-1)},

(283)

where
{\cal Y}_{(IJk),0}^{\{H,H\}}
contains all the full-depth terms, while
{\cal Y}_{(IJk),2}^{\{H,H\}}
and
{\cal Y}_{(IJk),3}^{\{H,H\}}
are proportional to
\pi^{2}
and
\zeta(3)
, respectively. Then, the discontinuity around the symbol letter
\omega_{i}
is given by

\displaystyle\text{Disc}_{\omega_{i}}\left[{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}\right]=\mu\left\{\left(\text{Disc}_{\omega_{i}}\otimes\text{id}\right)\left[\Delta_{1,2}{\cal Y}_{(IJk),0}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}\right]\right\}+\pi^{2}\text{Disc}_{\omega_{i}}\left[{\cal Y}_{(IJk),2}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}\right],

(284)

where the operator
\mu
maps the tensor product to the ordinary one. By requiring the vanishing of the discontinuities,

\displaystyle\text{Disc}_{\alpha_{IJ}+y_{IJk}}\left[{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}\right]=\text{Disc}_{1+\alpha_{IJ}y_{IJk}}\left[{\cal Y}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\},(-1)}\right]=0\,,

(285)

we fix the remaining coefficient.

Finally, we obtain the result of the first two orders of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}
in the
\epsilon
expansion,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{H,H\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg[-\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}U_{1}(y_{IJk})-2\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\log(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg]\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg(2V_{1}(\alpha_{IJ})+M_{100}(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}U_{2}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})\\
&+2\log^{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)-\frac{2}{3}\log^{3}\left(y_{IJk}\right)-\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(286)

D.3.2
Multiple-gluon-exchange webs

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211aHH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211bHH.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1}{4\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}\left[I_{(IJ)(Ik),1}^{\{H,H\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)-I_{(IJ)(Ik),2}^{\{H,H\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}I_{(IJ)(Ik),3}^{\{H,H\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\right]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(287a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121bHH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121aHH.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&-\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1}{4\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}\left[I_{(IJ)(Ik),1}^{\{H,H\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)-I_{(IJ)(Ik),2}^{\{H,H\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{1}{\epsilon}I_{(IJ)(Ik),3}^{\{H,H\}}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\right]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(287b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aHH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bHH.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&-\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{y_{IJk}}{4\epsilon}I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}(y_{IJk})+\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{1}{4\epsilon}I_{(kI)(kJ),2}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}(y_{IJk})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(287c)

The Feynman parameter representation of the master integrals is

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJ)(Ik),1}^{\{H,H\}}=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}\left[da\right]a_{2}\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt\left[\frac{a_{2}\left(a_{3}\alpha_{IJ}+a_{5}\right)\left(a_{5}\alpha_{IJ}+a_{3}\right)}{\alpha_{IJ}}+a_{1}\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)^{2}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+2a_{5}\right)\right]^{-2(\epsilon+1)}\,,\end{split}

(288a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJ)(Ik),2}^{\{H,H\}}=\,\,&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}\left[da\right]a_{2}\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt\left[\frac{a_{2}\left(a_{3}\alpha_{IJ}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)\left(a_{4}\alpha_{IJ}+a_{5}\alpha_{IJ}+a_{3}\right)}{\alpha_{IJ}}+a_{1}a_{5}^{2}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+2a_{5}\right)\right]^{-2(\epsilon+1)}\,,\end{split}

(288b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(IJ)(Ik),3}^{\{H,H\}}=\,\,&-2^{4\epsilon+3}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}\left[da\right]a_{4}^{2}\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt\left[\frac{a_{2}\left(a_{3}\alpha_{IJ}+a_{4}\right)\left(a_{4}\alpha_{IJ}+a_{3}\right)}{\alpha_{IJ}}+a_{1}a_{4}^{2}+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+2a_{4}\right)\right]^{-2(\epsilon+1)}\,,\end{split}

(288c)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}[da]a_{4}\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right){}^{3\epsilon}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt[a_{1}a_{4}\left(2a_{5}+a_{4}\right)+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}\right)+a_{2}a_{3}\left(2a_{5}y_{IJk}+a_{3}\right)]{}^{-2(\epsilon+1)},\end{split}

(288d)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I_{(kI)(kJ),2}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}[da]a_{3}\left(a_{1}a_{2}\right){}^{3\epsilon}\\
&\hskip-42.67912pt[a_{1}a_{4}\left(2a_{5}+a_{4}\right)+4a_{1}a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}\right)+a_{2}a_{3}\left(2a_{5}y_{IJk}+a_{3}\right)]{}^{-2(\epsilon+1)}\,.\end{split}

(288e)

The result of the function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{-\frac{1}{2\epsilon^{2}}\left[M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\right]\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[2G\left(-1,0,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-4G\left(-1,1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+2G\left(0,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+4G\left(0,1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-4G\left(0,\frac{1}{2},0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-4G\left(1,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&-4G\left(1,1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+4G\left(1,2,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+4G\left(1,\frac{1}{2},0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&-4\log(2)G\left(0,\frac{1}{2},\alpha_{IJ}\right)-4\log(2)G\left(1,2,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+4\log(2)G\left(1,\frac{1}{2},\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{6}\log^{3}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\log(2)\log^{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)-2\log^{2}(2)\log\left(1-\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{2}\pi^{2}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\pi^{2}\log\left(1-\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}+1\right)\\
&+\log(2)\left(M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+V_{1}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\right)-\frac{9\zeta(3)}{2}+\frac{2}{3}\pi^{2}\log(2)\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(289)

The function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}
is symmetric under the
(I,J)
interchange, so the sum of the two webs is vanishing,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(IK)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=0\,.

(290)

The remaining function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left[\frac{1}{3}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+\frac{11}{6}\pi^{2}\right]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(291)

D.3.3
Summary for
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}

The integration order of the master integrals appearing in this region is summarized in table
9
.

Integral

Cheng-Wu

Integration orders

I_{(IJ)(Ik),1}^{\{H,H\}}

a_{4}=1-a_{5}

a_{1}\rightarrow a_{2}\rightarrow a_{5}\rightarrow a_{3}

I_{(IJ)(Ik),2}^{\{H,H\}}

a_{4}=1-a_{5}

a_{1}\rightarrow a_{2}\rightarrow a_{5}\rightarrow a_{3}

I_{(IJ)(Ik),3}^{\{H,H\}}

a_{4}=1

a_{2}\rightarrow a_{1}\rightarrow a_{3}

I_{(kI)(kJ),1}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}

a_{4}=1

a_{5}\rightarrow a_{2}\rightarrow a_{3}\rightarrow a_{1}

I_{(kI)(kJ),2}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}

a_{3}=1

a_{5}\rightarrow a_{1}\rightarrow a_{4}\rightarrow a_{2}

Table 9
:
The set of master integrals of the
\{H,H\}
region associated with the tripole colour structure, specifying the integration measure in the middle column and the order of integration in the rightmost column.

Note that the integrals of the connected web are computed using differential equations, and we do not collect them here.
Finally, the result of the region function
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{H,H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\bigg\{\frac{1}{\epsilon^{3}}\log(y_{IJk})\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\bigg[-\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}U_{1}(y_{IJk})-2\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\log(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg]\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg(2V_{1}(\alpha_{IJ})+M_{100}(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1-y_{IJk}}{1+y_{IJk}}U_{2}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})+2\log^{2}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt-\frac{1}{3}\log^{3}\left(y_{IJk}\right)+\frac{7}{6}\pi^{2}\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(292)

Appendix E
Computation and result for UV regions at two loops

In this section, we will summarize the results of the UV regions in the two-loop calculation. We will use the same method as we introduced in appendix
B
, and follow the same structure as in appendix
C
. Regarding the calculation of the integrals, we will provide the Feynman parameter representation and the order of integration of the master integrals. We will also present the result of each web
{\cal Y}^{R}
in the region
R
as well as the region function
{\cal R}^{R}
, which is the sum of all the webs contributing to that region.

E.1
Region
{\cal R}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk)}

E.1.1
Connected web

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}=\,\,&\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111HUVCN.pdf}}}\\
=\,\,&-\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1}{12\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}\left[I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})-I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]\\
&+\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{(1-2\epsilon)^{2}(4\epsilon-1)}{12\epsilon^{2}(3\epsilon-1)}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\left[\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),2}(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),2}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(293)

The Feynman parameter representation of the master integrals is

\displaystyle\begin{split}I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})=&-4^{2\epsilon+2}\Gamma(2\epsilon+2)\int_{a>0}[da]a_{4}\left(a_{1}a_{5}\right)^{3\epsilon}\\
&\bigg[a_{1}\left(a_{4}+4a_{5}\right)a_{4}+a_{5}\left(a_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}\right)\\
&+\left(\alpha_{IJ}+\frac{1}{\alpha_{IJ}}\right)a_{5}a_{3}a_{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{1}a_{3}a_{4}\bigg]^{-2(\epsilon+1)},\end{split}

(294a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),2}(y_{IJk})=&-4^{2\epsilon}\Gamma(2\epsilon)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{4}\right)^{-2+3\epsilon}\\
&\left[a_{4}a_{2}^{2}+a_{1}a_{3}\left(a_{3}+4a_{4}\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{1}a_{3}a_{2}\right]^{-2\epsilon}.\end{split}

(294b)

The result of the function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{\frac{1}{6\epsilon^{3}}-\frac{2}{3\epsilon^{2}}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(\alpha_{IJ})\\
&+\frac{1}{12\epsilon}\bigg[-\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}4M_{100}(\alpha_{IJ})+\frac{1}{3}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+5\pi^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(295)

E.1.2
Multiple-gluon-exchange webs

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121aCNHUV.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/121bCNHUV.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1}{12\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk})+{\cal O}(\lambda),\end{split}

(296a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211bCNHUV.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/211aCNHUV.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&-\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{\alpha_{IJ}^{2}+1}{12\epsilon\alpha_{IJ}}I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(\alpha_{IJ},y_{IJk}^{-1})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(296b)

We expressed the result in terms of a master integral
I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),1}
that has been computed in the context of the connected web; see the parametric representation in eq. (
294a
). We directly write down the result of the function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}
here,

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{-\frac{1}{3\epsilon^{3}}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon^{2}}\left[{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{1}{3}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)}-\frac{1}{6}M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\right]\\
&+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\bigg[-\frac{4}{3}G\left(-1,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{4}{3}G\left(-1,0,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{4}{3}G\left(-1,1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{4}{3}G\left(0,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{4}{3}G\left(0,1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{4}{3}G\left(1,-1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&+\frac{4}{3}G\left(1,0,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{4}{3}G\left(1,1,0,\alpha_{IJ}\right)-\frac{1}{9}2\log^{3}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\\
&-\frac{17}{18}\pi^{2}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{1}{9}\pi^{2}\log\left(1-\alpha_{IJ}\right)+\frac{1}{9}\pi^{2}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}+1\right)\\
&+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{1}{6}M_{100}\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\log\left(y_{IJk}\right)}-\frac{1}{6}\log\left(\alpha_{IJ}\right)\log^{2}\left(y_{IJk}\right)+\frac{\zeta(3)}{3}\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(297)

Most of the terms in
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}
are
(I,J)
symmetric, while only the two terms in red are antisymmetric. Therefore, the sum of the two webs is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(Jk)(JI)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}+{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJ)(Ik)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}=\\
&\hskip-113.81102pt\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{\frac{2}{3\epsilon^{2}}\log(\alpha_{IJ})+\frac{1}{3\epsilon}M_{100}(\alpha_{IJ})\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,.\end{split}

(298)

E.1.3
Summary for
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}

The integration order of the master integrals appearing in this region is summarized in table
10
.

Integral

Cheng-Wu

Integration orders

I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}

a_{4}=1

a_{5}\rightarrow a_{1}\rightarrow a_{2}\rightarrow a_{3}

Table 10
:
The set of master integrals of the
\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\}
region associated with the tripole colour structure, specifying the integration measure in the middle column and the order of integration in the rightmost column.
The remaining integral
I_{(IJk),2}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}
is simple, and has been evaluated directly in
AmpRed
.

Finally, the result of the region function
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{\frac{1}{6\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{1}{12\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{1}{3}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+5\pi^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}\\
&+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(299)

Notice that the terms multiplied by
\frac{1+\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}{1-\alpha_{IJ}^{2}}
are completely canceled between the two webs.

E.2
Region
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}

E.2.1
Connected web

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CUVHa.pdf}}}+\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/111CUVHb.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{(1-2\epsilon)^{2}(4\epsilon-1)}{12\epsilon^{2}(3\epsilon-1)}\left[\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(y_{IJk})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(y_{IJk}^{-1})\right]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda)\,,\end{split}

(300)

The Feynman parameter representation of the master integral is

\displaystyle\begin{split}I^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(y_{IJk})=&-4^{2\epsilon}\Gamma(2\epsilon)\int_{a>0}[da]\left(a_{1}a_{4}\right)^{-2+3\epsilon}\\
&\left[a_{4}a_{2}^{2}+a_{1}a_{3}\left(a_{3}+4a_{4}\right)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}a_{1}a_{3}a_{2}\right]^{-2\epsilon}\,.\end{split}

(301)

Note that this integral is the same as
I^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}_{(IJk),2}(y_{IJk})
; see eq. (
294b
). The result of the function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(IJk)}^{\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{\frac{1}{6\epsilon^{3}}+\frac{1}{12\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{1}{3}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+5\pi^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(302)

E.2.2
Multiple-gluon-exchange webs

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aCUVH.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bCUVH.pdf}}}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112aHCUV.pdf}}}-\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[width]{fig/112bHCUV.pdf}}}\right)\\
=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}{\cal N}^{2}\frac{(1-2\epsilon)^{2}(4\epsilon-1)}{6\epsilon^{2}(3\epsilon-1)}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{y_{IJk}}}I^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(y_{IJk}^{-1})-\sqrt{y_{IJk}}I^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(y_{IJk})\right]\\
&+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(303)

The result is expressed in terms of the master integral,
I^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}_{(IJk),1}(y_{IJk})
, that has already appeared in the connected web in eq. (
300
); see the parametric representation in eq. (
301
). The result of the function
{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathcal{Y}}_{(kI)(kJ)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{-\frac{1}{3\epsilon^{3}}-\frac{1}{6\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{1}{3}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+5\pi^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}\\
&+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(304)

E.2.3
Summary for
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}

The master integral
I_{(IJk),1}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}
is simple, and has been evaluated directly in
AmpRed
.
The result of the region function
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}
is

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}=\,\,&\left(\frac{\alpha_{s}}{4\pi}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{m}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{-2\epsilon}\log(y_{IJk})\bigg\{-\frac{1}{6\epsilon^{3}}-\frac{1}{12\epsilon}\bigg[\frac{1}{3}\log^{2}(y_{IJk})+5\pi^{2}\bigg]\bigg\}\\
&+{\cal O}(\epsilon^{0})+{\cal O}(\lambda).\end{split}

(305)

Notice that
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{\mathrm{UV}},H\right\}}
is exactly
{\cal R}_{(IJk)}^{\left\{C_{N},H_{\mathrm{UV}}\right\}}
(eq. (
299
)) with an opposite sign. As a result, UV regions do not contribute at the correlator level.
